## Snapshot
SK hynix is the world's #1 high-bandwidth memory (HBM) maker and the #2 DRAM/NAND producer overall, and is **NVIDIA's primary HBM supplier**. Near-monopoly HBM3E history — its HBM tool/process share into NVDA was ~100% "until last fall" (SemiAnalysis HBM). UBS models 2026 industry HBM share at **SK Hynix / Samsung / Micron = 56% / 22% / 22%**, with SK hynix "already shipping HBM4" and larger volumes by 2Q26 (UBS 2026 overview, Dec-2025) — i.e. share normalizing off the historical ~90% as Samsung/Micron qualify. FY2025 revenue 97.1tn KRW (+47% YoY), operating profit 47.2tn (+101%), 49% OP margin (SK hynix IR FY25, 2026-01-29). Mix: DRAM (incl. HBM) is the profit engine; NAND/enterprise SSD via wholly-owned **Solidigm**, where the group's eSSD share reached 30.2% in Q4-2025 (search digest, 2026-06); SK Hynix+Solidigm is modeled at ~36% of 2026e QLC eSSD output, the largest single bloc (MS AI NAND overview). It sits at the demand epicentre of the AI capex cycle: HBM has been "sold out since 2023," 2026 DRAM/HBM volume is contracted out (Q3 2025 call, 2025-10-29), and vendors reaffirm the 2026 sold-out/allocation tone (DeepResearch TSM, citing SK hynix/Micron commentary).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) HBM (HBM3E/HBM4 — #1, profit engine) · 2) DRAM (DDR5/LPDDR/GDDR7) · 3) NAND / enterprise SSD (Solidigm) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | NVIDIA (primary HBM customer, into Rubin; ~56% 2026 HBM share, non-NVDA capped ≤20%); other hyperscalers/ASIC; mobile & PC OEMs |
| **Key suppliers** | ASML (EUV litho), AMAT/LRCX (tools), silicon wafers; Hanmi (TC-bonding for HBM packaging); TSMC (HBM4 logic base die) |

## Position in the value chain
SK hynix converts silicon wafers and litho/etch/deposition tools (ASML EUV, AMAT, LRCX) into DRAM + HBM stacks and NAND/eSSD, then ships to NVIDIA (primary HBM customer, into the Rubin platform), other accelerator/hyperscaler buyers, and mobile/PC OEMs. Its moat is HBM process + advanced packaging lead (HBM3E 12-high, first-to-mass-production HBM4) plus a net-cash balance sheet (35tn KRW at Q1-2026) funding the M15X / Yongin / Indiana packaging build-out. On DRAM node mix it also leads Samsung: ~40-50% of SK hynix DRAM bits on 1a-nm-or-more-advanced nodes vs Samsung ~30% (Micron 70%+) — a key reason for Samsung's relative de-rating (Bernstein Semis deck, May-2025). HBM packaging hinges on a few specialist tools: SK hynix's thermocompression (TC) bonding for HBM3E 12-high has run almost entirely on Hanmi tools; a Q2-2025 dispute (Hanmi pulled field service) showed how concentrated this dependency is, with ASMPT/Besi/Hanwha alternatives not yet qualified (SemiAnalysis HBM).

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕 2026-08-12 — THE KIOXIA STAKE IS CONFIRMED AS INDIRECT (VIA AN SPC), BOTH NAMES TRADED UP ON IT, AND KOREA IS ATTRACTING SOVEREIGN CAPITAL INTO THE MEMORY COMPLEX.** (Jefferies Asian TMT End of Day / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-12 — sales & trading, not Research.)

BofA hosts KIOXIA IR on 2026-08-18 — the venue to ask them.**

This seems abnormal" — expecting SEC's HBM ASP to more than double in 2027 with HBM4 share reaching 44% (largest)** (Jefferies / Jeff Kim via Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-11). ➜ **A 44% HBM4 share for Samsung is only reachable at someone's expense, and on the existing splits that someone is primarily SK Hynix. ⚠️ NOT ADOPTED — it must be reconciled against the HBM4 share splits already on this page and on [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) before use, and it comes from the house that covers the competitor.**

**🆕 2026-08-09/10 — the -15% week, and JPM's rebuttal (JPM / Jay Kwon, 2026-08-09; OW, PT W2,750,000 Jun-27; price W1,416,000 at 07-Aug).** SKH fell **15% in the week to 07-Aug (vs Kospi -5%, SEC -9%)** on three headlines, all of which JPM pushes back on:
- **HBM "50% discount" reports are inaccurate.** JPM's own assumption is **below +40% y/y HBM ASP from 2026E** — deliberately conservative — on three grounds: (1) suppliers prioritise **DDR5/LPDDR5/NAND LTAs, which carry a margin premium over HBM**; (2) NVDA is SKH's largest customer with multi-year sourcing leverage; (3) HBM re-prices annually, so a soft 2027 print is recoverable. Upside risk to EPS if SKH lands above.
- **W54tn ($38.1bn) infrastructure capex disclosed:** **W35.2tn for a DRAM plant at Yongin Y2** (vs W31tn for Yongin Phase 1; groundbreaking **July 2027**, mainly capacity **beyond 2031**) and **W19.1tn for NAND at Cheongju M17** (groundbreaking February, first cleanroom **end-2028**, investment through April 2031). JPM reads it as fab-space securing after the **1mn-wafer-by-2030E** target, not a demand call.
- **Shareholder return pulled forward:** per a 08-07 DART filing SKH will finalise and announce additional shareholder-return measures **by 3Q26-end** (previously "within year-end" at the 2Q26 briefing). JPM models **cumulative FCF >W800tn over three years** and flags a **16-20% two-year yield potential for SEC/SKH** (Global Memory note, 08-09).
- **Solidigm US IPO: "under review," reconfirm within one month** (DART, 08-05). JPM is cautiously negative on the logic — NAND margins top 70%, the balance sheet funds capex without dilution, and a listing may collide with **Korea's duplicate-listing rules** from the early-2026 commercial-code change.

**Next company-specific catalysts (JPM, 08-09):** (1) shareholder-return program update **by Sep-end**; (2) **HBM contract price update by Sep-end**; (3) **US subsidiary listing decision within one month**.

**🆕 2026-08-10 — Chongqing packaging stake: "no decision made."** Responding to a KRX inquiry, SKH said no decision has been taken on a reported **~W4tn (~US$3bn) stake sale in its Chongqing, China packaging plant**, that it is "reviewing various options to strengthen the competitiveness of its packaging business," and will disclose within one month (regulatory filing via Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-10; DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10). Separately, SKH faces **a second USITC investigation from MonolithIC 3D covering HBM and 3D NAND patents** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10).

**Q2 2026 (reported 2026-07-28) — first headline miss of the cycle, but in line with where the sell-side had already cut.** **Operating profit ₩60.54tn vs consensus ₩64.2tn**; **revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected** (Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-07-28). Crucially, the "miss" is against a stale consensus: **local Korean brokers had already cut operating profit to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' own Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so O'Mara's read is that the print "shouldn't be coming as much of a shock" — it is in line with the most recent numbers rather than with the screen consensus. **Profitability remains extreme: GM 83%, operating margin 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — "all pretty healthy despite the perceived miss." Sequentially this is still a step up from Q1's ₩52.6tn revenue / ₩37.6tn OP, i.e. the *level* accelerated even as the *beat* disappeared.

**What management said (Q2 2026 call, 2026-07-28, via Jefferies):**
- **DRAM:** Q2 bit growth **up high-single-digits with ASP +30% q/q**; **2026 DRAM bit growth guided to mid-20s%**; **Q3 bit growth +10%, focused on server products.**
- **HBM4:** **shipments began in Q2** at customer-required speeds, **ramping further in 2H**; **1cnm-based HBM4E samples supplied to a major customer in 1H**. Jefferies attributes the revenue shortfall specifically to **"the delay in the ramp of HBM4."**
- **SOCAMM2** modules "grew significantly" in Q2 and are **fully commercialised on 1C**.
- **Market view:** the scope of memory demand is **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** and spreads across services — AI-server sophistication driving structural demand across **HBM / server DRAM / eSSD**; improvements in models and software efficiency expected to *expand* AI usage and infrastructure demand rather than reduce it. Big-Tech customers are expanding infrastructure investment and memory procurement on surging AI service growth and a **shortage of computing capacity**. **PC and smartphone sales saw a temporary adjustment due to challenges securing memory volumes**, with recovery expected as supply tightness eases.
- **Capex:** full-year capex guided to rise **as much as 80% to the high-₩40tn range** (Culpium · Tim Culpan, 2026-07-28) — a very large step-up and, per Jefferies, being **pulled in**. **No buyback announced despite a large net-cash position** (Jefferies, 2026-07-28).

**Net-new from the full call transcript (Bloomberg FINAL, call 2026-07-28, ingested 2026-07-29)** — the primary source behind the relays above:
- **The bottom half of the P&L is a Kioxia story: net non-operating profit ₩62.2tn**, driven by **₩63.3tn of gains from the sale and valuation of investment assets** (the Kioxia stake sale, per the closing Q&A) + ₩1.1tn FX gains → **pretax ₩122.7tn, net profit ₩93.9tn, net margin 118%** (net > revenue). Balance sheet after it: **cash ₩88tn (+₩33.6tn q/q), net cash ₩69.4tn, D/E 7%**; D&A only ₩4tn; EBITDA ₩64.6tn (81%).
- **LTA framework, primary detail: negotiations CONCLUDED with ~10 customers including key customers**, more in discussion; terms "normally around **five years**"; pricing "not uniform" — a range of mechanisms designed to address price volatility; and **deposits incorporated** "to support contract fulfillment and enhance the visibility and reliability of customers' mid-to-long-term demand plans." Management won't say what share of sales LTAs will cover — "an appropriate level." (This is the demand-side half of the LTA/deposit chain the page already carries tool-side via ASML's prepaid EUV orders — BofA, 2026-04-15.)
- **NAND, the underreported half of the print:** ASP **+mid-50% q/q**; **eSSD revenue 2x q/q**, Solidigm 30TB+ eSSDs **3x q/q**; Q3 bit guide only **+low-single-digit** (vs DRAM +~10%); **321-layer to ~50% of domestic capacity by YE** as planned; industry demand high-10s% (vs DRAM mid-20s%). Strategy framing: AI storage is moving **inference-centric — eSSD as a core tier of the AI memory hierarchy**, with **KV-cache offloading and "near-GPU storage"** named as the new-tier products and QLC eSSD the HDD-replacement/AI-data-lake solution; "it will not be a single SSD that handles all the workload."
- **DRAM ASP mix, management's own explanation** (the +30% that disappointed): "shipments of some high-value-add products were pushed back into the second half" — 2H bit growth guided **above 1H**, with HBM4 ramp + 1c conventional DRAM lifting blended ASP; sales mix managed by "demand visibility, long-term customer relationships and supply-demand dynamics," not short-term price.
- **HBM roadmap specifics:** HBM4 yield/quality "**nearing the levels of HBM3E**, which is already in the maturity stage" — focus now on ramping capacity; **HBM4E volume production from 2027** (samples delivered 1H); beyond hybrid bonding, **iHBM for HBM5-class products integrates cooling elements in-package, cutting thermal resistance >30%**. **2027 HBM volume + pricing negotiations are underway and "progressing smoothly"** — and management concedes the conventional-DRAM price surge "may also have some influence on our HBM pricing discussions" (while listing wafer intensity, TSV/packaging capacity and opportunity cost as the real pricing inputs) — i.e. the commodity super-cycle is a tailwind, not a cap, to HBM pricing.
- **Capex drivers behind the high-₩40tn guide, stated:** M15X mass-production schedule **pulled forward** + investments to expand capacity fast after the **Yongin Fab1 clean-room opening in early 2027**; mid-to-long-term: **P&T7 (advanced packaging) + M17 (new NAND base)** + a **new domestic semiconductor cluster beyond Yongin** — staged against "customer demand visibility and investment efficiency." On overseas (US/Japan) expansion chatter: siting will be decided on power/water/talent/supply-chain/customer access, but "**at this time there are no further decisions made** in addition to the investment that has already been announced."
- **Demand-debate answer (to JPM's leasing/efficient-models question):** DC leasing + efficient models are "not a sign of AI investment slowdown but a transition toward **higher utilization of infrastructure already built at scale** and accelerated monetization"; efficiency "lowers price and adoption barriers," evidenced by "explosive demand for the recent high-efficiency AI models"; **"our major customers are still requesting more memory supply."** Project timing may slip on "physical constraints such as power availability and data center construction," but investment stays solid "beyond next year." **PC/mobile: "temporary sales adjustments" because device makers can't SECURE memory** — the shortage is rationing the consumer end.
- **Oversupply pushback (to the capacity-expansion question):** expansion is "based on visibility secured in partnerships with customers" (LTAs), executed "flexibly in alignment with confirmed customer demand" — "we do not believe our medium-to-long-term investment plans will lead to oversupply **right away**."
- **ADR mechanics:** the July-10 Nasdaq listing was "the **largest ever for a foreign company's IPO in the US**"; **from July 30 ADRs freely convert into Korean shares, but the reverse is limited** — stock→ADR conversion needs regulatory filings ("several weeks") and total ADRs are capped at the **17,790,000-share conversion limit** (= the offering size); raising the ADR proportion "no decisions made." (Confirms and details the @jukan05 07-14 fungibility item in Catalysts.) **Shareholder returns:** additional measures under review "from multiple angles," to be communicated **within the year** — with disclosure currently constrained by ADR-offering regulatory restrictions (the stated reason the print carried no buyback).

⚠️ **The tape around this print matters more than the print.** SK Hynix reported into the worst two sessions of the year for Asian semis: on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and a circuit breaker (the eighth of the year), Samsung -12% and SK Hynix -13%**, on two things landing together — **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% (China's second-largest IPO ever) making the China memory threat feel immediate**, and Citadel flagging a live Fed hike with swaps pricing ~40% odds of 25bps (Jefferies "Silk Road," 2026-07-28). On the print itself the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell another ~4% after hours on top of an 8.9% decline during the session** (Jefferies, 2026-07-28). Culpium's framing of the day: *"SK Hynix Sales and Profit Miss, Bears Roar."*

📌 **The pricing-discipline question this print reopens.** Jefferies: the miss "is just going to fuel the view that **Hynix has been less aggressive than Samsung on pricing given its commitments to [[NVDA]] on HBM**" — i.e. the NVIDIA relationship that is this page's central moat argument is now also being read as a pricing constraint. That sits directly against the sector call from Jefferies research the same morning: **memory price hikes are tracking 15-20% versus the 25-30% expected**, 2027 consensus requires **~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, and **memory prices peak around 1H27** (Jefferies "Silk Road," 2026-07-28) — with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).

🎯 **The pre-print bogey — and MS's explicit call that the reaction, not the number, was the test (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28).** Recorded ahead of the print ("we do have earnings for Hynix tomorrow. And we have Samsung on Thursday"), MS's Asia memory host laid out where expectations sat: **"for Hynix the numbers were anywhere between 60 to 65 trillion. Our number is currently at 65 trillion. We've seen some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** Against the **actual ₩60.54tn OP**, that puts the print **at the very bottom of the buyside range and ~7% below MS's own ₩65tn** — i.e. the drift toward "the 60 handle" that Sean flagged was directionally right and the houses that had not cut were the ones marked. More useful than the number is the framing he attached to it: **"I think the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** Read against what actually happened — SK Hynix **opened -6% and recovered to -2.3%** while [MU](MU.md) fell ~8.9% intraday and a further ~4% after hours — the reaction test came back **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for Hynix specifically** and clearly negative for the complex. On the buyback the print did not deliver, Sean's pre-print read was non-committal: **"For Hynix, my view is that it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that."** He also placed the name at the centre of the CXMT event: CXMT closed its Shanghai debut at a **US$492bn market cap, the 25th most valuable company in the world**, and **"you can probably say that that's quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix"** — an explicit framing of the CXMT re-rating as a *transfer* from the incumbent, though he bounds the fundamental risk to the outer years ("probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years"). (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html)

⚙️ **The second-order read on the HBM4 delay: less HBM means MORE commodity DRAM, and it is now showing up in the industry bit number (Morgan Stanley webcast · Sean, 2026-07-28).** Sean attributes part of the upward revision in **TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth — from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year, and ~24% next year** — to **"Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result"** (alongside [Samsung](SAMSUNG.md)'s P4 second-floor cleanroom). This is the supply-side mirror of the revenue miss already on this page: **the same HBM4 ramp delay that cost the top line is simultaneously freeing wafer into commodity DRAM at a moment when commodity-DRAM margins are competitive with HBM** — which is precisely the "HBM-vs-EPS paradox" this page carries from MS's Sean Kim (04-14: "it's better if you don't have HBM" because commodity-DRAM returns are now higher). Two-sided, and the page should hold both: the delay is a **share/mix negative on the NVDA franchise** and a **near-term bit/EPS cushion**, and it is also **incremental industry supply** feeding the cycle-durability bear. On NAND the analogous driver is YMTC (third line, **50,000 wafers** next year), taking NAND bit growth from high-teens to **20-25% next year**. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28)

**Q1 2026 (reported 2026-04-23, English call):** Revenue **52.6tn KRW (+60% QoQ, +198% YoY)**, operating profit **37.6tn** (≈doubled QoQ), **OP margin 72%** (+13pp QoQ), net profit 40.3tn (77% net margin), EBITDA 41.3tn. Net cash 35tn KRW (cash 54.3tn). Management: AI-infrastructure demand drove "significant price increases for both DRAM and NAND"; customers "prioritize securing volume over price." HBM4 demand "far exceeding current supply capacity," supplying HBM3E + HBM4 simultaneously. NAND shipments ~-10% QoQ on a high prior-quarter base (SK hynix IR / Yahoo Q1 FY26; CNBC, 2026-04-23). **Trajectory across the 4 saved calls:** OP margin 41% (Q2'25) → 47% (Q3'25) → 58% (Q4'25) → 72% (Q1'26) — a vertical margin ramp on HBM/DRAM ASP.

**Capacity build-out — narrowing the DRAM gap, dominant in HBM (StoneX/TrendForce "Memory expert call — Avril", 2026-06-23):** SK Hynix exits 2026 the #2 DRAM maker at **~610K wafers/month** (vs Samsung ~740K) but reaches **710K by end-2027, narrowing the gap to Samsung** (which adds only ~45K). It remains the **dominant HBM supplier with the largest HBM wafer cap at ~200K end-2026** (vs Samsung 180K, Micron 100K). **Yongin FAB1 (200-250K, geometry-dependent)** is expected ready end-2027, but chip output only lands in 2028 given equipment install (~1-1.5 quarters) plus the 2-quarter wafer-in-to-wafer-out cycle; **Yongin FAB2 was just announced** and is "far along." Clean-room space — not equipment or demand — is the binding constraint through end-2028. **Risk:** Samsung's improving HBM4 raises a real possibility it overtakes SK Hynix as #1 HBM supplier in 2027 (same source; cross-ref SAMSUNG). **Anthropic** is pursuing a deal with SK Hynix (server DRAM + eSSD 2027 / HBM 2028), implying an in-house Anthropic ASIC (same source).

**HBM4 cadence / pricing (expert detail, Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17):** >70% of SK hynix's 2026 HBM bits are still HBM3 (12-high crossover hit in April); **HBM4 does not cross over in 2026 — blended ≤50% of mix**, ramping in 2H26. HBM pricing "finalized" and tracking at start-of-year expectations, with SK hynix guiding **HBM ASP +40-50% YoY in 2026**; cannot renegotiate HBM3E prices and there is "more upside in demand but limited capacity." Blended HBM margin can dip in 1H26 (less HBM4 in the mix), then hold once HBM4 is mainstream. SK hynix frames **~70% OP margins as the level "needed to maintain R&D," and "not high enough"** to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding capex step-up — pushing back on the peak-margin bear narrative. It is capacity-rationing non-NVDA: with limited M15X output it prioritizes HBM4 and "cannot increase non-NVIDIA above 20%" in 2026, having declined ASIC/AMD volume (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).

**Rubin (R200-class) HBM supply split; HBM4 engineering-sample specs; Yongin Phase 1 timing; 2026 DRAM WFE capex (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06):** SemiAnalysis sizes the first-12-months Rubin HBM supply split at **SK hynix ~60% / Samsung ~30% / Micron ~0%** — SK hynix's HBM3/HBM3E execution has become the industry benchmark for supplier differentiation. On HBM4 engineering samples, SK hynix is running **~10 Gbps pin speed** (in line with Samsung, ahead of Micron), with **lower jitter and stronger package execution than Samsung's** competing part. On capacity, **Yongin Phase 1's cleanroom does not come online until Feb-2027**, with only limited wafer output starting 3Q27 — but the **M15X fab and Yongin Phase 1 schedules have both been pulled in/accelerated** versus prior timelines. On capex, SemiAnalysis models **2026 DRAM WFE capex growth of +34% at SK hynix — the highest of the three** (vs Samsung +26%, Micron +20%). SemiAnalysis notes it "turned bullish" on Samsung in its November note while maintaining its existing bullish view on SK hynix (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania: How a Once-in-Four-Decades Shortage Is Fueling a Memory Boom", 2026-02-06).

**HBM4/HBM4E primer — SK hynix the lead supplier (Atlas Peak Research "HBM4 and HBM4E: The Memory Wall Becomes the System Architecture Problem," 2026-04-03):** JEDEC finalized HBM4 in April 2025 — **2048-bit interface / 32 channels / 2.048 TB/s per stack** at the 8 Gb/s baseline (~2x HBM3E's 1024-bit / 1.229 TB/s), up to 64 GB/cube; HBM4E extends to **16 Gb/s / ~4.096 TB/s**. SK hynix **shipped 12-layer HBM4 samples in March 2025 and completed HBM4 development in September 2025**, with **NVIDIA Vera Rubin the key platform** and HBM4E readiness targeted **~2027**. At **GTC 2026 SK hynix demonstrated a custom cHBM design with a "stream DQ" architecture** that continuously feeds data to compute units in a queue-based structure — the base die becoming a semi-custom integration layer is "the next competitive frontier." Corroborates SK hynix's HBM4/Rubin lead and the value-capture-into-base-die/packaging thesis this page tracks (Atlas Peak, 2026-04-03).

**MR-MUF packaging moat, all-around power TSVs, and the Hanmi/Hanwha bonding-tool drama (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** SK hynix's proprietary **MR-MUF** (mass reflow molded underfill, co-developed with NAMICS) gives better thermal dissipation than the NCF process Micron/Samsung use, and sidesteps thermal-compression bonding (TCB) entirely in favor of batch mass reflow — more productive at volume. On HBM3E, SK hynix moved to **all-around power TSVs (vs. two banks previously)** — roughly 6x more TSVs, cutting IR drop for VPP by up to 75%. This packaging lead is also SK hynix's single point of tool-supply fragility: **Hanmi held ~100% share of TC bonders at SK hynix until fall 2024**, when Hynix diversified and ordered from **Hanwha**; Hanmi responded by **pulling its field-service teams from SK hynix's fabs in early April 2025**. The Hanwha tools then went undelivered, and the **ASMPT bonders Hynix had ordered in fall 2024 turned out not to work for HBM3E 12-high** — forcing SK hynix into the position of placing a **placating order back with Hanmi** to keep HBM3E 12-high packaging running. SK hynix also **lacks in-house advanced-logic design capability** (like Micron), so it depends on TSMC plus design/IP partners for the HBM4 custom base die (N12/N3 variants). Its proprietary TSV layouts are framed as a core moat, with the potential to **charge licensing fees** for TSV-layout exposure going forward; NVIDIA's rising HBM content is described as **primarily benefiting SK hynix** (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11).

**The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off — the supplier-side read from the AMD launch, and a two-tier HBM4 market (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** AMD's MI455X ships **12 stacks of HBM4 = 432GB per package** at **23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks = 288GB**, Rubin at **22 TB/s** — a near-tie on bandwidth *despite AMD running a 50% wider bus*, because AMD clocks its HBM4 at a pin speed of **7.6 Gbps while Nvidia targets 10.7 Gbps, 40% faster**. SemiAnalysis is explicit this was deliberate and supplier-facing: "**Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification. This change was precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X.**" The cost landed on the memory makers — the change "**was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and the standing implication is that **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD** for the same generation. AMD and Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. **Read-through for SK hynix, the #1 HBM supplier and Nvidia's primary source:** an above-JEDEC pin-speed target means **tighter binning, lower effective yield on the top bin, and a widening quality/price spread** between suppliers that can hit 10.7 Gbps and those that cannot — a **share-and-mix question, not a volume question**. It also means HBM4 is **two markets, not one**: the AMD 7.6 Gbps socket is a materially easier bin than the Nvidia 10.7 Gbps socket, so being short of Nvidia's spec locks a supplier out of the *premium* bin rather than out of HBM4. Two page-level cross-checks worth holding together, carefully: (1) this page already carries SemiAnalysis's own datapoint that SK hynix's **HBM4 engineering samples ran ~10 Gbps** with **lower jitter and stronger package execution than Samsung's** ("Memory Mania", 2026-02-06) — which sits just under the 10.7 Gbps Rubin target, i.e. the rework SemiAnalysis describes is precisely the kind of gap the industry had to close; and (2) the "**issues have been finally resolved**" language is the first explicit statement on this page that the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is **behind** the supply chain. **SemiAnalysis does not say which supplier wins the 10.7 Gbps bin, and nothing here should be read as saying SK hynix does** — the useful conclusion is that the binning spread itself is now a live differentiator between the three suppliers, on top of capacity. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)

## Debate / thesis
- **Bull — structural HBM/DRAM supercycle, sold-out book.** "Sold out 2026" confirmed at DRAM + HBM level (Q3'25 call); tight supply expected into 2027; HBM4 in mass production ahead of NVDA Rubin (initial HBM4 shipments with a TSMC-fabbed logic base die in 2H26, DeepResearch TSM). HBM4 itself is a step-up, not a commodity refresh: 2048-bit bus (2x HBM3E) lifting bandwidth ~1.5x to ~1.6TB/s, and a custom logic base die from HBM4 onward that strengthens vendor lock-in via proprietary TSV layouts and lets memory makers earn a higher GM (a "corkage" charge for customer-supplied base dies) — with only SK hynix and Micron viable suppliers (SemiAnalysis HBM). JEF (Conor, 2026-04-07) flagged a Jefferies KBI note arguing Korea memory can out-earn the hyperscalers; SK Hynix ~20% pullback called a buy opportunity (JEF Titu/Wong, briefing 2026-06-19). DRAM ASP +50-60% QoQ in 2Q26, +20% QoQ 3Q26; NAND +75-100% QoQ in 2Q (memory complex table, briefing 2026-06-19). @rwang07 (Ray Wang): "SK Hynix is the next 3 trillion-dollar company" (briefing 2026-06-19).
  - **Jefferies' "Korea out-earns Nvidia" thesis quantified (Conor, 2026-04-07):** Jefferies' KBI analyst (Jeff Kim) put out a note arguing **Samsung will earn more operating profit than Nvidia next year** yet trades at ~1/5 the market cap — and by read-across the same HBM-pricing economics flow to SK Hynix (Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07). The mechanism: **HBM4 is ~35% more expensive than HBM3E**, and (per a Dec-2025 Jefferies check) Hynix's HBM operating margins are flat rather than the −20% feared — i.e. NVIDIA/hyperscalers are absorbing the 35% HBM4 price hike (same call). The Jefferies' bull-vs-bear framing: there is "massive disagreement on memory," with one camp (incl. Jefferies) seeing a mid-cycle correction and another seeing "no cycle whatsoever" — both can be true on timing; the rally so far has been ASP-led and now needs to convert to **unit growth into 2027** since hyperscaler memory spend already runs ~30-35% of their operating cash flow (Samsung's own framing of the ceiling — Jefferies "Asia tech", 2026-04-07).
  - **HBM4 base-die supply chain (BofA TSMC expert, 2026-04-09):** the TSMC ecosystem supplies the **logic/communication base die under HBM** to SK Hynix (and Samsung joining the alliance); the expert framed NVIDIA's Jensen Huang as the supply-chain "firefighter" personally chasing Hynix capacity — corroborating Hynix as the primary HBM source into Rubin (BofA "Best ideas", 2026-04-09).
  - **Memory-led TAM expansion underpins the ASP thesis (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15):** BofA's Global Semis team raised its **2026 semiconductor market forecast from ~$1.0tn to ~$1.3tn**, "the vast majority driven by higher ASPs for memory" — explicitly lifting the **DRAM market size from just below $200bn to ~$400bn**, and introducing a **2030 semi market of ~$2tn** (vs ASML's own ~$1tn+ CMD-Nov-24 framing). For the litho read-through, ASML's analyst raised 2027/28 EUV-for-DRAM deliveries by 4 systems (2 Samsung, **2 Hynix**); **Korea was 45% of ASML Q1 revenue, +40% YoY** — the clearest tool-side confirmation of the Korean DRAM/HBM capex ramp (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
  - **Hynix is the gating item on the Rubin ramp (JPM/Gokul TSMC preview, 2026-04-02):** the Rubin output ramp (Q3 first output, big Q4 step-up) is "**limited by the availability of HBM4 from the three vendors, and Hynix is the one which is a little bit more of a limiting factor right now — especially the high-speed versions**." JPM caps **2026 Rubin at ~2–2.5mn units max**, with NVIDIA offsetting the HBM4 constraint by raising **Blackwell/GB300** orders — i.e. Hynix HBM4 yield/speed is the binding constraint on NVIDIA's flagship volume (JPM "TSMC preview", 2026-04-02).
  - **Longevity-of-cycle re-rate (GS, Junie Yoon):** GS argues the cycle's longevity is underestimated — Hynix at 5-6x earnings means the market disbelieves durability; GS's 9x target is "conservative" vs peers with similar margins/moats at high-teens-to-20x. Even a bear-case DRAM −30%/yr in 2027-28 leaves DRAM GM ~40%, supporting an earnings (not P/B) multiple; demand visibility lets suppliers spend capex efficiently, lowering the odds of an overbuild-driven crash vs past cycles (GS "Memory", 2026-06-08).
- **Bear — it's still a commodity, peak-margin valuation, share normalizing.** Ex-Micron VP procurement (2026-06-10 expert call): HBM becomes a commodity once 2-3 vendors are qualified; "your $900 stock becomes a $50 stock again" once capacity normalizes — risk inside CY26/early CY27. Jefferies (Conor, 2026-04-07): mid-cycle correction risk, rising data-center concentration. **Share erosion is now in the numbers, not just feared:** UBS models SK hynix HBM share at 56% in 2026 (vs Samsung/Micron 22%/22%), well below the historical ~90% (UBS 2026 overview, Dec-2025). Samsung "believes it has the best-performing HBM4" (1C, internally approved, samples to all customers, mass production/full-scale sales targeted 1H26) and SK hynix itself notes **NVIDIA is encouraging Samsung to ramp** to relieve tightness for Rubin Ultra (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17; Samsung targeting NVDA HBM4 qual by 1Q26 — UBS 2026 overview). MS stays **EW on SK hynix "until the HBM margin-erosion debate gets fully priced in"** (MS AI NAND overview). Risk that elevated DRAM/NAND pricing destroys PC/mobile demand (Q1'26 call; Tim Cook WSJ "once-in-100-year flood," briefings 2026-06-18/19).
- **The HBM-vs-EPS paradox (MS, Sean Kim, 2026-04-14):** on the headlines that Hynix may reduce HBM4 volume/share, MS's call is that it **doesn't matter for EPS — "it's better if you don't have HBM" because commodity-DRAM returns are now higher** — but HBM matters for the *multiple* (DRAM trades ~4-5x peak earnings vs HBM ~10x). So lower HBM share is EPS-neutral-to-positive but multiple-negative (MS "Memory", 2026-04-14). NAND is framed as the more interesting industry than DRAM (two AI drivers — KV-cache offload + AI storage; early S-curve penetration; ~6-7yr NAND fab payback vs ~2yr DRAM, so little incentive to add NAND capacity) (same call).
- **Hynix-vs-Samsung flow dynamics (JPM, Jay, 2026-05-28):** JPM's call that Samsung should "catch up" with Hynix post-labor-strike "didn't work" — Hynix's recent outperformance is tied to **single-stock-ETF + passive index buying centered on Hynix**, plus the confirmed US listing/ADR catalyst and improving HBM4 progress; retail is positioned toward Hynix. (Jefferies, 2026-04-07, frames the US-listing valuation kicker as the main Hynix driver — but flags the offsetting "sell Micron, buy Hynix" arbitrage as a reason the PB gap may not be as wide as bulls think.)
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands — 🆕 THE DIRECTION REVERSED ON 2026-08-04: two houses resumed/updated coverage on the SAME DAY, both stayed bullish, and BOTH CUT THEIR PRICE TARGETS BY DE-RATING THE MULTIPLE WHILE RAISING OR HOLDING EARNINGS.** **JPM (Jay Kwon) OW, PT W3,000,000 → W2,750,000**, target multiple **8x → 7x F12M P/E**, timeframe extended **Dec-26 → Jun-27**, with **FY26E-28E EPS RAISED 3-16%** — the cut is explicitly about patience, not numbers: *"the road to a multiple normalization (FTM P/E from <4x to 6x or above) will take time in the current market environment. Reflecting higher opportunity cost vs. the past… in the third year of the AI capex race."* **BofA (Simon Woo) Buy, PO W3,590,000 → W3,000,000**, multiple **9x → 8x 2027-28E P/E**, with the bull case intact (**W300tn+ annual OP run-rate from 3Q26, ~4x 2027-28E P/E, no hard landing**) — and **a NEW instrument: the ADR initiated at PO US$250, a 20% premium to the local shares.** ⚠️ **Read the pair together: after a May-June wave of synchronized PT HIKES on the LTA re-rating thesis, the same analysts are now marking down what the market will pay for those earnings. Nobody downgraded. The earnings thesis is unchanged-to-better; the re-rating thesis has been pushed out. That is the single clearest sell-side signal on this page in three months.** **Street range now: Arete W4,300,000 (07-21, Street-high) · Jefferies W4.2m · ⚠️ Mirae W2,800,000 (Buy maintained; CUT from W4.2m on 2026-07-29 — this page carried the stale W4.2m until 2026-08-12, see [## Changelog](#changelog)) · BofA W3.0m · JPM W2.75m · MS W2.6m (bull W3.0m / bear W1.1m) · UBS W2.25m (the standing dissenter — removed from APAC Key Call, top pick switched to Samsung).** _(Historical detail below.)_ No SK Hynix-specific broker email in inbox to date for the earlier period (Outlook unavailable in that environment — user to append). **A synchronized wave of PT hikes hit May-June 2026 as the LTA framework drove a P/B→P/E re-rating call:** BofA Buy W2.1m→W3.0m (May) →W3.59m (Jun 22); JPM OW W1.8m→W3.0m (May 17, 8x FY26/27 EPS); MS W1.7m→W2.6m (May 18, ~38% upside — a shift more constructive than its earlier "EW until margin-erosion priced"); Barclays OW €0.9→€1.1 (May 3, 6x); UBS Buy W1.8m→W2.25m (May 20) — **UBS the notable dissenter, removing SK Hynix from its APAC Key Call list post-outperformance and switching its top pick to Samsung** (Samsung to reach equal HBM share by '27). Research-report reads: UBS bullish on memory tightness but flags share normalization (SK Hynix HBM 50% 2026 → ~40% level-with-Samsung 2027); Jefferies expert channel constructive on HBM pricing/capacity discipline; SemiAnalysis structurally constructive on the HBM4/custom-base-die value-capture duopoly. Closest internal reads: JEF buy-the-dip; GS (Schneider) bullish memory tightness "through CY27" via the MU preview read-across (briefing 2026-06-19); MS (Nowak) >$1trn hyperscaler capex '27 underpins memory demand (briefing 2026-06-18). **Arete (Nam Hyung Kim) Buy, PT W4,300,000 (2026-07-21)** — the Street-high on the page (above Mirae/Jefferies W4.2m); constructive on the M17 NAND greenfield (~$53bn, ~150k wpm NAND by '30-31, incl. Solidigm) within a still-tight NAND market (net capacity <10% growth by '30) — see the 07-21 Intra-quarter row + [hbm-memory theme](themes/hbm-memory.md).
- **History corroborates the current share lead — SK hynix took HBM3 share from Samsung, and is staying conservative on HBM4 node choice (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11):** SK hynix **took HBM3 share from Samsung** as Samsung's front-end process shortfalls emerged during the Nvidia Hopper era — the origin of SK hynix's current dominant HBM position. For HBM4, SK hynix is **staying on the 1b node** rather than jumping to the riskier 1c node Samsung is attempting; SemiAnalysis describes SK hynix's yields as "progressing smoothly," at the same level as prior generations at the same development phase — a lower-risk, steadier path than Samsung's leapfrog attempt (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: The Rise and Roadmap of HBM", 2025-08-11).

Thesis identical to Samsung: DRAM structural P/E rerating ("valued on P/E not P/B, and by this measure still look cheap") + Jevons Paradox (cheaper tokens → more compute demand → higher DRAM prices). SK Hynix framed as a "picks and shovels" primary beneficiary of the AI capex arms race. Bull signal (Jefferies / Christopher Wood "GREED & fear: The mother of all cycles", 2026-06-25). Implies **doubling installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years** and pulling the Yongin fab ramp forward **7-12 years**; memory execs say supply is only **~50% of demand and worsening**; JPM sizes a **US$12-20tn cumulative revenue opportunity** for Korean memory makers (same source). JPM separately estimates **60-70% of the $3.1tn is front-end wafer spend, 20-30% infrastructure + cleanroom** — a long-term positive read for SPE, power infra, semi EPC suppliers, and memory makers (JPM · Jay Kwon, Intl Market Intelligence, 2026-06-30). Market reaction: Hynix shares **+3% in Asia trading** on the news (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily, 2026-06-30).
- **"Hyperscaler curse" — Korea capex-beneficiary basket outperforms even as the memory names themselves sell off (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-01; Jefferies Silk Road · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-01).** Despite the $3.1tn capex-plan news, SK Hynix fell **-2%** in Korea trading on 2026-07-01 (Jefferies · Cristina Titu) and **-3.4%** per Rothschild, part of a **nine-day Korea foreign-selling streak** and a broader Korea underperformance vs Nasdaq/SOX strength (KOSPI -1.8%, Jefferies). US semi-cap names (AMD/AMAT/LRCX/KLAC) hit 12-month highs on the same DRAM-capacity-addition narrative; JPM dubs the disconnect the "**hyperscaler curse**" — the Korea capex-beneficiary basket up 5% even as memory names sold off, attributed to NPS (Korea pension fund) rebalancing and a dip in June DRAM export data (Rothschild & Co TMT Daily · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-01).

The current Bernstein PT is **KRW 3,300,000**; the 1,150,000 is retained here only as the note's stale body figure._ Memory read-through of the note: **DRAM adopts High-NA EUV first, at the 1d node in 2027** — Bernstein's clearest early signal is SK Hynix, which announced in Sept-2025 it had assembled ASML's TWINSCAN EXE:5200B (the first production-oriented HNA system) at its M16 fab for next-gen DRAM development; SK Hynix and Samsung DRAM are Bernstein's expected first HNA adopters (2027, 1d), ahead of logic. Bull signal on the litho-leadership angle (Bernstein · David Dai, "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML__High-NA_EUV_cost_deep_dive_-_lifting_PT_to_2300_on_ri.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/ASML__High-NA_EUV_cost_deep_dive_-_lifting_PT_to_2300_on_ri.html)
- **MS reiterates OW — SK hynix a key AI/agentic-AI beneficiary (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02).** In its Global Perspectives, MS (Shawn Kim) maintains **OW on SK hynix** (alongside OW Samsung, its DRAM Top Pick) as a **key beneficiary of AI compute and agentic-AI trends**, estimating **>20-30% DRAM price hikes in 3Q26** — enough to keep the YoY rate of change accelerating and support P/E stability at **5.2x 2027e earnings**. Caveat consistent with the page's durability debate: **YoY pricing likely plateaus in 4Q26** vs supply-discipline visibility only clearing into 2028, so the stocks may lack near-term cyclical catalysts until 2028+, though multiples can keep re-rating on favorable LTAs. MS order-of-preference PT: **KRW2,600,000** (bull KRW3,000,000 / bear KRW1,100,000) (Morgan Stanley "NAND Industry Outlook", 2026-07-02). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Nand_-_MS.html)
- **Korea "Three Mega Investment Projects" — MS economist detail: SK hynix nearly DOUBLED its commitment (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01).** MS Chief Korea/Taiwan economist Kathleen Oh details the June-29 plan (a distinct, more granular read on SK hynix than the JPM framing already on the page). Under **Mega-Project I (semiconductor clusters)**, SK hynix announced it will invest **KRW1,100tn to build an AI-memory production network** — **KRW600tn in the Yongin cluster, KRW100tn in Chungju, KRW400tn for a new southwestern-region cluster** — and aims to **complete the 4th Yongin fab by 2033, accelerated 12 years from the prior 2045 schedule**. Under **Mega-Project II (AI data centers)**, SK hynix **"nearly doubled its commitment in building new AI data centers, adding another KRW1,000tn to its capex plan"** (part of the SK Group / GS / Naver joint 15GW-by-2035 initiative, 8.4GW by 2029, ~KRW550tn govt-projected). SK hynix's explicit targets: **double DRAM capacity within 5 years and reach ~3x current wafer capacity by 2034**. Yongin Fab 1 Phase 1 opens Feb-2027; execution the key risk (Morgan Stanley "South Korea: A Landmark Industrial Strategy", 2026-07-01). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260701_Morgan_Stanley_035420-KS_South_Korea-_A_Landmark_Industrial_Strategy_Unveiled_.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260701_Morgan_Stanley_035420-KS_South_Korea-_A_Landmark_Industrial_Strategy_Unveiled_.html) _(This ≥KRW2,000tn/$1.5tn "Three Mega Projects" govt-plan headline is a distinct framing from the JPM ~$3.1tn combined-corporate-capex-through-2040 figure already on the page — same underlying June-29 announcement, different scope/aggregation; both logged.)_

- **ADR-premium / flow-donor read + 4Q DRAM ASP check (Jefferies (sales) · Rankie Wong, 2026-07-09).** Jefferies sales say investors are comfortable with a **15-20% SKHY ADR premium** (the TSM ADR comp now ~13%, 10-yr avg 10-15% — above MS S&T's ~5-10% estimate on the page); post-correction **MU trades <5x P/E on buyside $200 EPS — roughly on par with Hynix**, so **MU is seen as the likely flow donor once SKHY lists**, with Samsung (MSD P/E) a catch-up candidate. Latest checks (Sherman/Edison) point to **4Q DRAM ASP +30-40% q/q, higher than most expect**; memory called "the most crowded trade globally." (mixed — bullish ASP/flow vs crowded-positioning caveat) (Jefferies · Rankie Wong, 2026-07-09).
- **Bear counterpoint on output growth + leverage (Tim Culpan / Culpium, 2026-07-08).** Culpan flags SK Hynix's **bit-shipment CAGR at only ~11% since 2020** — lagging Samsung on output growth over the past two AI years — and a **highly levered balance sheet** (contrast the page's net-cash W35tn Q1-2026 framing); a bear read on whether the HBM-mix lead has come at the expense of overall bit-output competitiveness. (bear) (Tim Culpan / Culpium, 2026-07-08).
- **ADS roadshow — LTA strategy is margin-not-floor, and the structural case for a higher P/E (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06).** On the ADS roadshow management drew an explicit contrast with Micron: asked whether it would adopt Micron's floor-price LTAs, mgmt said "my main focus is margin… rather than the pricing arrangement," structuring each customer's LTA differently. The re-rating logic laid out directly: the new LTAs run **3-5 years with penalty provisions** (vs the old ~1-yr "gentleman's agreements" that were unenforceable — "you cannot sue basically") → lower demand/supply-shock volatility → a higher P/E deserved; a higher HBM mix (annual contracts vs quarterly/monthly commodity pricing) reinforces the lower-volatility case. **>50% of the 2028-30 capacity expansion will be LTA-covered** (its major LTA customers are >50% of revenue), with **LTA signing expected before year-end 2026**. On capacity, total wafer capacity is guided to **~2x by 2030, but customer demand for 2030 is "a lot bigger" than that potential supply**. Management **pushed back on any committed HBM-to-DDR margin parity** ("to my best knowledge, we haven't committed anything") — the current strong market is only a positive backdrop for next year's HBM price negotiation, framed around preserving core-customer relationships. On China, **CXMT has ~20% of DRAM wafer capacity but only ~6-7% of bits** (low yield → worse quality, usable only for low-end consumer), and China memory impact has been "minimal"; YMTC's expansion has repeatedly under-executed on Western-tool access. **ASIC share of HBM is "going up a little" but the majority of HBM still goes into GPU.** Bull on the durability/re-rating leg (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_06_sk_hynix_roadshow.html)
- **Chairman Chey Tae-won — the strategic case: capacity-doubling, partnership moat, and "memory-as-a-service" (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10).** Around the ADR debut (priced **$149/ADR** vs an indicated **$177**, raising **$26.5bn**), SK Group Chairman Chey framed the AI memory cycle as a structural break from the old boom-bust: the LTAs were **proposed by customers, not SK Hynix** ("not SK… they actually require that"), so "it's not cyclical business anymore." SK Hynix has announced it will **double total capacity within five years**, but customers "said that's not enough… they want us to quadruple, five times, six times." Supply tightness lasts **until AI "settles" with human society** (no fixed date); the demand cycle is now driven by agentic-AI KV-cache, not device-unit counts. On the "AI bubble": **the technology is real (no bubble); only the stock market may overshoot**. Two durable moats: SK Hynix **works with TSMC on the HBM base die "which the others do not do,"** and **"doesn't compete with any customers"** — both enabling deeper partnerships. Also: a new **US fab in Indiana**; **SK Group ~$1T AI-datacenter investment over 10 years (~15GW in Korea + ~5GW outside Korea)**, with **~$35B already invested in the US** (bio, battery, Hynix, SKT AI startups); and a longer-term **"memory-as-a-service"** concept (memory delivered as a managed solution rather than just product). Bull/strategic framing (Bloomberg TV — Chairman Chey Tae-won interview, 2026-06-10). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_10_ceo_hynix_interview_10jun26.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_10_ceo_hynix_interview_10jun26.html)
- **JPM server-CPU model — rising DIMM/SOCAMM demand a fresh read-through for SK Hynix (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, 2026-07-12).** JPM's new proprietary server-CPU shipment model forecasts total server CPUs from **26mn units 2025 → 68mn 2028 (38% CAGR)**, with overall server shipments sustaining **20%+ growth**, driven by AI head-node CPUs (AI-accelerator shipments ~50% CAGR FY25-28) plus a **155%-CAGR "agentic-AI" server-CPU** vector (inference orchestration / test-time scaling). JPM frames the agentic-AI server "super-cycle" as **positive for memory vendors SK Hynix and Samsung Electronics on rising DIMM/SOCAMM demand** (SK hynix OW, W2,221,000 as of 10-Jul; no new PT in this note). Corroborates the server-DRAM/SOCAMM demand leg already on the page (NVDA Vera CPU, LPDDR5/SOCAMM pull). Bull read-through (JPMorgan · Albert Hung / Gokul Hariharan, "server CPU shipment model", 2026-07-12). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Server_JPM_proprieta_2026-07-12_5362742.html)
- **Mirae Asset maintains Buy, TP W4,200,000 — "take a broader view," pullback = attractive entry (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim "SK Hynix — Take a broader view", 2026-07-14).** Mirae **maintains Buy and TP W4,200,000** (unchanged, last raised 06-25; current W1,845,000 on 7/13 → **127.6% upside**) — ties Jefferies (07-06) as the **street-high TP on the page** — arguing the recent ~14% 1-month pullback reflects a "sharp unwinding" of 2Q26/ADR-listing optimism that is "already priced in." **It CUT earnings forecasts ~12%:** 2Q26F OP to W62.3tr (from W70.7tr); **2026F OP W266,817bn (from Mirae's prior W299.1tr) vs consensus W275,793bn**; **2027F OP W388,855bn (+46% YoY)**; 2028F OP W400,069bn; OP margin **76.8%/76.6%/73.5%** (26/27/28F); revenue W347.5tr/W507.9tr/W544.1tr; **EPS 26F W295,026 (+400.4% growth), P/E 6.3x**; capex **W56.9tr/W79.7tr** (26/27F, 2026 unchanged). LTAs cover **~50% of revenue** (earnings-stability + less speculative-demand exposure). Industry backdrop "exceptionally tight": **DRAM 16Gb DDR5 & DDR4 spot up 40+ consecutive trading days**, near prior highs even with the CXMT-listing overhang; 2027 growth driven by HBM lifting overall pricing as LTA/HBM-committed capacity crowds out conventional DRAM. TP = **6.5x target P/B** (10% discount to Micron/Kioxia pure-play avg) on 12MF BPS W583,213. Bull signal (Mirae Asset · Young-gun Kim, 2026-07-14). _Divergence: the W4.2m Mirae/Jefferies street-high sits far above JPM OW **W2,221,000** (07-10), Bernstein W3,300,000 (07-06), UBS W3,200,000 (07-03), MS W2,600,000 (07-02), BofA W3,590,000 (06-22) — an unusually wide ~W2m PT dispersion across the desk._ [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_SK_Hynix_000660_KS_Buy.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260714_SK_Hynix_000660_KS_Buy.html)
- **BofA rebuts the 2Q ASP-miss fear — "DRAM ASPs look better than concerns" (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-13).** Into the sharp mid-July memory selloff (triggered by the KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview ~8% below the W65tn consensus), BofA argues the correction is **overdone**: conventional DRAM ASP is **+50-60% QoQ**, Korea semiconductor exports **+193% YoY in the first 10 days of July**, and DRAM spot has rallied since June. BofA assumes global DRAM ASP **+53%/+21%/+7% QoQ for 2Q/3Q/4Q26**, and frames **Korea's lower 2Q ASP (~low-to-mid 40% QoQ) as 2Q-specific** — a product of stable HBM contract prices plus an exceptionally high 1Q ASP base — **not** the start of a 3Q deceleration. A direct counter to the SK Hynix ASP-miss narrative (higher HBM mix → lower blended ASP hike) behind the plunge. Bull signal (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-07-13).
- **Record Seoul plunge then a same-week rebound to near all-time highs — a flow/ETF whipsaw, not a fundamentals re-rate (Bloomberg / @citrini / Barron's / @firstadopter, 2026-07-13→14).** Days after the record-setting Nasdaq debut, SK Hynix posted its **biggest drop on record in Seoul** on the KIS/local 2Q26 OP-preview miss ("SK Hynix Shares Plunge Most on Record in Deepening Korea Selloff," Bloomberg via Outlook 2026-07-13; @zerohedge -4.5% 07-14; @citrini flagged **W1,850 as the technical line** below which leveraged Korean retail gets "wiped out"). It then **rebounded ~+27% the next session to approach all-time highs as the US ADR premium kicked in** (The_AI_Investor / @firstadopter / Barron's, 07-14). @JulianKlymochko: "$SKHY is a $1 trillion company trading like a meme stonk" — **3.8x next year's earnings, +493% over 12 months**, "the most contrarian long in the market" (07-13/14). @jimcramer: "How did all of tech become hostage to SK Hynix!" — leverage to "ridiculous leveraged ETFs" (07-14). (valuation, bear→mixed — sentiment/technical washout on the ASP-miss headline, reversed as ADR-premium/flow reasserted.)
- **Price up, content down — the 2027 HBM-pricing call and the offsetting de-spec, sourced from the buyer's own BOM (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24).** The bull leg is unusually well-placed because it comes from the *accelerator vendor's* bill of materials rather than a memory maker: SemiAnalysis explicitly expects "**a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM**" — commodity-DRAM margin is now the *anchor* HBM is being repriced toward, which is the same inversion this page tracks from the other side (Redburn: HBM 2027 revised down 60→50bn Gb-eq because conventional DRAM is more profitable; roadshow 07-06: management explicitly **refused to commit** to HBM-to-DDR margin parity). And it is modelled as strong enough to **compress the accelerator vendors themselves**: AMD's ASPs must rise but it cannot pass the full memory increase through, because "most of AMD's volume goes to large buyers with real negotiating leverage, and those buyers have more alternatives than they used to," so HBM cost passes through "**at a lower incremental margin than their corporate margins, dragging down gross margins on the MI455X package.**" That is a third-party estimate of HBM pricing power strong enough to move a customer's gross margin — the most direct external support yet for the page's "next year's HBM price negotiation" framing. Baseline: "**HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025**" (SemiAnalysis Memory Model / Accelerator and HBM Model). **The offsetting bear leg is in the same note and is the reason price × prior content is the wrong revenue model.** Tight supply is already destroying attach: the original 2025 MI455X BOM estimate carried "a staggering **138TB in DRAM across LPDDR5X and DDR5 MRDIMM**"; the current estimate has **LPDDR5X entirely removed** plus a **significant DDR5 de-spec to a far lower DDR5 RDIMM content** — specifically the roadmapped **up to 1TB of direct-attached LPDDR per MI455X accelerator on the EAM module has disappeared**, attributed directly to tight memory supply. SemiAnalysis's summary is the line to carry: "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" Net for SK hynix: **price-led revenue with content as a live offset** — bullish on ASP durability into 2027, but the bits-per-accelerator assumption underneath consensus volume is now the exposed variable. (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026", 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html)
- **Jefferies reiterates BUY on both Korean memory names (Jefferies (Silk Road) · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-15).** Samsung and SK Hynix both rated **BUY** in the Silk Road note — consistent with the standing Jefferies bull stance (W4.2m TP, 07-06). Bull signal (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-15).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **Q2 2026 earnings:** late July 2026 (English call; prior Q2 was 2025-07-24) — HBM4 ramp progress, DRAM ASP, capex number.
- **HBM4 / NVDA Rubin ramp** through 2026; HBM4 blended ≤50% of mix in 2026, 2H26 crossover (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); whether SK hynix holds ~56% HBM share (UBS 2026 overview) vs Samsung's pursued NVDA slot.
- **Samsung/Micron HBM4 qualification at NVDA** — Samsung targeting NVDA HBM4 qual by 1Q26 (UBS 2026 overview); the single biggest share-erosion risk, with NVDA itself nudging Samsung to add capacity (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Rubin (R200-class) HBM supply-share split** — whether SK hynix holds SemiAnalysis's modeled ~60% of first-12-months Rubin HBM supply (vs Samsung ~30%, Micron ~0%); confirmation would validate the current HBM3/HBM3E-execution lead carrying into HBM4 (SemiAnalysis "Memory Mania", 2026-02-06).
- **2026 capex disclosure & funding** — M15X (Cheongju) ramp delayed by Korea labour-law/industrial conflicts (fab structure targeted mid-2026, fab start ~later; Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17); first Yongin cluster fab delayed to next year; Indiana advanced-packaging expansion; dollar HBM capex rising sharply (greenfield + hybrid bonding) — SK hynix is "open to customer deposits" to help fund it. **Roadshow use-of-proceeds detail (SK Hynix ADS roadshow, 2026-07-06):** the ~$26.5bn ADR proceeds fund **Cheongju (₩19tn, plant PNT7) + Yongin (₩21tn) + EUV scanners**; net cash since Q3'25, management **targets ₩100tn cash by year-end 2026** ("easily") and, having hit that position earlier than planned, will **announce a shareholder-return measure on the Q3 conference call** (executable this year, vs the original 3-yr plan). New **M17 (Cheongju) NAND capacity contributes from 2029** (the reason NAND expansion in the roadshow deck footnotes read as ~2030 — decided/disclosed after the deck was produced). **Yongin Y1 acceleration (@jukan05, 2026-07-14):** SK hynix has begun placing equipment orders for Yongin Y1 and **pulled the Phase-1 pilot ("one-pass") line forward to Feb-2027 (from May-2027)**, with full install toward ~20K wpm following in Mar/Apr on **1c DRAM** (the most advanced node in commercial production, also feeding HBM4E, which could commercialize as early as next year); it also **moved equipment price-negotiations forward a quarter (to 3Q)** to speed installs. Y1 Phases 2&3 cleanroom construction is set to start 2H-2026; the Yongin cluster (KRW600tn / 4 fabs) has its 4th fab pulled forward 12yrs to 2033 — concrete execution on the capacity pull-forward.
- **ADR/local arbitrage "malfunctioning" until July 29 (@jukan05 / Korea Securities Depository, 2026-07-14):** two-way conversion between SK Hynix's Korean-listed common shares and the Nasdaq-listed SKHY line **only becomes possible after July 29**, when the newly issued Korean common shares backing the ADR offering list domestically. Until then the fungibility mechanism is inoperative — a technical explanation for the ADR-vs-local premium dislocation in the interim window.
- **Korea national AI mega-plan follow-through** — Samsung + SK Group's **KRW4,755tn (~US$3.1tn)** long-term capex plan through 2040 (JPM · Jay Kwon, 2026-06-30, revised up from the $880B/$1.3T framing reported just 48hrs earlier); watch for confirmation of the implied doubling of installed Korean DRAM capacity within 5 years and the 7-12yr Yongin pull-forward, plus whether the 60-70% front-end-wafer / 20-30% infra-cleanroom capex split (JPM, 2026-06-30) holds as disclosures firm up. Near-term signal to watch: whether the "hyperscaler curse" disconnect (capex-beneficiary basket +5% while memory names sell off — Rothschild, 2026-07-01) resolves toward the memory names as NPS rebalancing/DRAM-export-data noise fades.
- **US Nasdaq listing — expected Friday 2026-07-10** (fresh capital raise on top of the KRX:000660 line); investor reception is a read on both the IPO-market strength and the US-investor bid for the name (only MU among the big-3 is US-listed today). Firms up the "US listing/ADR" re-rating catalyst previously flagged by JPM (05-28) / Barclays (05-03) with a concrete date. On the valuation the listing highlights: 3.6x fwd sales vs MU 4.6x / NVDA 10.8x, P/B discount to MU (The Information, 2026-07-05). **Ticker confirmed SKHY** and the listing **raises capital** as part of the offering; rationale is to make the stock easier for US investors to buy; context cited: Hynix revenue **+200% between 2023 and 2025, then +200% again in Q1 2026** (The Information, 2026-07-05; multiple bookrunner IPO notices, 2026-07-06). **Size now put at ~US$29bn — potentially the largest-ever US IPO by a foreign issuer**, with proceeds "largely earmarked for expanding manufacturing capacity in Korea and securing additional EUV lithography machines from ASML" — "not financial engineering… a direct bet that the AI memory shortage is far from over" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). Trading setup: several desks recommend **buy the ADR / sell the Seoul-listed shares** (ADR expected to command a premium + a cleaner route for global HFs), and Korean authorities are reportedly preparing for significant FX flows ahead of settlement. Valuation framing: Hynix trades ~**7x this year's / 5x next year's earnings** vs AI stories at 30-80x sales — "extraordinarily difficult to describe as anything other than cheap" as long as AI capex is alive; the biggest AI winners so far have been the "picks-and-shovels" suppliers, "and among those nobody is better positioned than SK Hynix" (Jefferies · Cristina Titu, 2026-07-07). **Deal size marked at ~US$28.2bn** (~17.79M new shares / ~2.5% of TSO; 1 ADR = 0.1 common share) by Morgan Stanley (S&T) on the 07-08 marketing detail — consistent with Jefferies' ~US$29bn (rounding/pricing-window difference, not a supersession); see the dedicated ADR-mechanics bullet below for premium/flow/index detail.
- **US Nasdaq ADR listing — mechanics & premium/flow view (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing", 2026-07-08).** MS Equity S&T (Pankaj Mataney, Asia Platform Sales & QDS — *note: S&T commentary, not MS Research, no rating*) details the deal: SK Hynix began marketing the proposed **Nasdaq ADR (ticker SKHY)** on **2026-07-06**; offering ~**US$28.2bn** via ADRs representing ~**17.79M new common shares (~2.5% of TSO)**; **1 ADR = 0.1 common share (10 ADRs = 1 ordinary)**; bookbuild Jul 6–9, pricing Jul 9, **ADRs begin trading 2026-07-10**, subscription-proceeds payment ~Jul 14, new underlying shares list on KRX Jul 29. Proceeds fund domestic expansion (Yongin, Cheongju packaging, advanced equipment). **Liquidity:** MS estimates the ADR line at **~10-20% of aggregate turnover** near-term (foreigners ~30% of local turnover × ~50% US-based); plus leveraged ETFs **SKHU/SKUU (2x long), SKDD (-2x)** expected ~Jul 13 adding dealer-hedging/rebalancing flow (Asia-listed SK Hynix levered ETFs, ~$18.7bn AUM, already run ~18% of cash turnover). Most Korean ADRs stay <10% of turnover (ex-KT/SK Telecom at 31-61% on prior FOL breaches). **Premium:** MS expects the SKHY ADR to trade at a **~5-10% premium** to the local line (higher in event-driven windows like index/ETF inclusion) — between typical Korean ADRs (parity to low-single-digit) and TSMC ADR (double-digit; ~18% as of Jul 6 vs 13.3% 5-yr avg). Driver = limited ~2.5% issuance headroom + supportive narrative; caveat: headroom alone doesn't guarantee a premium (TSMC traded near parity in stretches), and weaker memory fundamentals/sentiment could compress it toward parity. **Fungibility is one-way:** ADR→local seamless (from Jul 29); local→ADR constrained by the ~2.5% headroom (expected fully used at launch), so new ADRs need existing holders to cancel/convert back. Further primary issuance is limited near-term — **SK Square must hold ≥20% of SK Hynix (currently ~20.5%)**, capping dilution; any expansion likely via secondary/buyback structures or onshore approval. MS also flags **KSD headroom (not SEC Form F-6, which shows max program size of 1.78bn ADS / 25% TSO) as the right benchmark** — some investors are mis-sizing US index-inclusion market cap off the F-6 figure. **Index events:** MSCI ~**$1.9bn** inflow (Nov-2026 review, local line); FTSE ~**$1.28bn** (Sep-2026, local line); **KOSPI 200 ~$955mn** (ad-hoc, effective ~Jul 29); **VanEck SMH ~$3.8bn** (earliest Sep-2026 if treated as an index IPO, else Sep-2027); **SOX ~$114mn** (earliest Sep-2027); **Nasdaq-100 near-term inclusion unlikely** (eligibility on full ADR market cap ~$30bn vs the ~$200bn fast-entry / ~$70bn top-75 thresholds). Options expected ~2 trading days after listing; SEC Rule 105 / Reg M short-sale constraints may reach the local line given fungibility (Morgan Stanley (S&T) "SK Hynix ADR Listing: Key Investor Questions and Our Views", Pankaj Mataney, 2026-07-08). [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_-_SK_Hynix_ADR_Listing_Key_Investor_Questions_and_Our_Views_1.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/MS_-_SK_Hynix_ADR_Listing_Key_Investor_Questions_and_Our_Views_1.html)
- **SKHY book heavily oversubscribed** — demand for the ~$28bn US share sale was **more than 7x available shares** (Reuters, 2026-07-09), a strong read on the US-investor bid ahead of the 2026-07-10 trading debut. (bull)
- **✅ RESOLVED — Nasdaq ADR debut (2026-07-10): priced at $149, raised ~$26.5bn.** SK Hynix priced its ADRs at **$149**, raising **~US$26.5bn** (regulatory filing) — the largest-ever first-time share sale by a foreign company on US markets, **pushing aside Alibaba** for the record (Bloomberg @business; Reuters, 2026-07-10). The book was **7x oversubscribed with 500+ investment firms**, and even key/anchor investors (incl. Leo/Philippe allocations) had **final allocations cut on the excess demand** (FT via @firstadopter, 2026-07-10). ADRs priced at a **~3% premium** to the local line (below the ~5-10% MS S&T range and the 15-20% some desks were comfortable with — @zerohedge, 2026-07-10; Barron's "set to trade at a premium"). Realized raise (~$26.5bn) came in a touch below the ~$28.2-29bn marketing size (MS S&T 07-08 / Jefferies 07-07). Korean-listed line still trades **~4.5x forward earnings** (FT via @firstadopter). ETF issuers have **≥6 products ready to launch next week** (incl. leveraged SKHU/SKUU/SKDD) (Bloomberg @business, 2026-07-10). Cramer: SK Hynix analysts likely to raise numbers Monday to "keep this tight" (@jimcramer, 2026-07-10). (bull — the US-listing/ADR re-rating catalyst is now realized with record-setting demand)
- **Intel Ohio fab — US-manufacturing option (@jukan05 / Korean media, 2026-07-21; SK Hynix official, 2026-07-22):** Korean media reported SK Hynix in talks / a "bold move" to acquire Intel's Ohio fab for US memory production. Both parties denied a sale/acquisition (07-21), without denying a possible JV; SK Hynix's official statement (07-22) says it "has neither pursued nor decided" on the Ohio plant but "continuously reviews various investment/acquisition opportunities." Watch item on US-footprint pressure — likely rumor for now, but a live channel for the US-localization push (cross-ref the Indiana packaging build).
- **HBF (High Bandwidth Flash)** — SK hynix signed an MOU with SanDisk (Aug-2025) to standardize HBF for AI inference (NAND augmenting HBM); first SanDisk HBF samples 2H26 (MS AI NAND overview).
- **MU print** as the cleanest near-term memory demand/pricing tell; UBS sees MU DDR GM surpassing HBM GM for the first time in early CY26 on commodity-DRAM tightness (UBS 2026 overview).
- **Consumer demand elasticity** — PC/mobile cuts (NVDA SOCAMM2 cut, AAPL prepayment/balance-sheet talk; briefings 2026-06-18/19).

## Risks
- **Commodity cyclicality / ASP reversal** — peak ~72% OP margin is not a through-cycle level; capacity additions (own + China CXMT/YMTC) normalize pricing. CXMT aggressively expanding HBM (HBM2 8-high mass production from 1H25, TSV capacity nearing Micron's by YE); Huawei building its own HBM supply chain (SemiAnalysis HBM).
- **HBM share loss / customer concentration** — UBS 2026 HBM share of 56% (vs ~90% historically); NVDA encouraging Samsung to ramp; Samsung's HBM4 self-described as best-in-class (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17; UBS 2026 overview).
- **HBM tool dependency** — concentrated TC-bonding reliance on Hanmi for HBM3E 12-high; ASMPT/Besi/Hanwha not yet fully qualified (SemiAnalysis HBM). Concrete case study: Hanmi held ~100% share of SK hynix's TC bonders until fall-2024 diversification into Hanwha; Hanmi then pulled its field-service teams from SK hynix's fabs (early April 2025), the Hanwha tools went undelivered, and the ASMPT bonders ordered fall-2024 didn't work for HBM3E 12-high — forcing SK hynix to place a placating order back with Hanmi to keep packaging output flowing (SemiAnalysis "Scaling the Memory Wall: HBM", 2025-08-11).
- **Demand destruction** — elevated DRAM/NAND prices crimp PC/mobile bit demand.
- **De-spec / content destruction in the accelerator platforms themselves — a distinct risk from consumer elasticity (SemiAnalysis, 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-21).** The platforms are cutting DRAM and HBM attach *because* memory is scarce and expensive, so bits-per-accelerator (which most models hold flat or growing) is the exposed variable rather than price. On AMD's MI455X the **up-to-1TB per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier has been removed outright** and DDR5 de-spec'd to a far lower RDIMM content vs an original 2025 BOM of **138TB of DRAM per platform across LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM** — "much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs" (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). In the custom parts it is a direct HBM-content cut: **Meta's custom MI450/MI455 halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4), cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package, and downgrades the HBM4 from 12-Hi to 8-Hi** — at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers, on a program SemiAnalysis argues "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin," making the read **doubly negative: fewer stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink** (SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset", 2026-07-21; restated 2026-07-24). Read against the page's LTA thesis this cuts both ways — take-or-pay locks volume for the covered book, but the *un*covered ~33-50% of the market is exactly where de-spec bites. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html)
- **HBM4 top-bin qualification, not just capacity** — Nvidia's above-JEDEC **10.7 Gbps** HBM4 pin-speed target (vs **7.6 Gbps** on AMD's MI455X) forced suppliers to rework HBM4 and means Nvidia "must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD"; that concentrates the risk in **yield on the premium bin** rather than in wafer output, and widens the quality/price spread between the three suppliers (SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat?", 2026-07-24). Relevant given this page's core swing factor is HBM4 allocation at NVIDIA; SemiAnalysis names no bin winner.
- **Capex intensity / funding** — large greenfield fab + packaging build-out (Yongin, M15X, Indiana) into a possible cycle peak; rising hybrid-bonding/R&D cost; Korea construction delays (Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Funding mechanism corroborated tool-side: ASML's DRAM customers only placed (prepaid) EUV orders **after receiving hyperscaler prepayments** — i.e. the LTA/deposit chain is what de-risked the capex (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Litho/tool intensity & EUV bottleneck risk** — Hynix's DRAM expansion is increasingly EUV-layer-intensive (1C ~6-7 EUV layers, a prospective 1D ~10 layers ~2028), but legacy capacity adds still pull **immersion**: e.g. **SK Hynix's Wuxi (China) fab runs no EUV**, so capacity growth there is immersion-driven — a China-exposure/tool-mix nuance (BofA ASML callback, 2026-04-15).
- **Korea/macro & FX** — KRW reporting, geopolitical/export-control exposure, China memory entrants.
- **Antitrust / price-fixing litigation** — a 17-plaintiff DRAM price-fixing class action filed in N.D. Cal. (2026-06-30) names Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron for allegedly conspiring to keep DRAM artificially scarce; early-stage legal/headline overhang (media report via @dnystedt, 2026-06-30).
- **Solidigm/NAND** — fragmented, structurally less profitable than DRAM, though MS sees Solidigm as a key NAND-upcycle beneficiary on high QLC eSSD exposure and fully-depreciated, low-cost fabs (MS AI NAND overview); HBM-driven DRAM mix-shift squeezing NAND wafers cuts both ways.

**Sinal vs gestão** — what management said on the Q2 2026 call × what the flow is saying (✓ confirma · ⚠ nuança · ✗ contesta):

_**Síntese — update 2026-07-30 (two days post-print, after the expert call and the Korea unwind):** the mechanical explanation for the "miss" is now settled and it is **mix, not execution**. The BTG former-SK-hynix executive: SK hynix runs **~40% of wafer capacity on HBM vs Samsung's ≤25%**, and HBM pricing is set roughly a year ahead, so when conventional DRAM spiked SK hynix's blended DRAM ASP printed **+30% against Samsung's +mid-40%** — Samsung captured the spot spike, SK hynix was contracted through it. Same source: **operating margin still went 72% → 76%**, "exceeding margins seen in companies like TSMC or Apple." The offsetting fact is the moat: **HBM4 yield ~80-85% at SK hynix (already at mature-generation levels) versus ~60% at Samsung**, which is what lets SK hynix supply Nvidia's Rubin volumes and is worth ~40% of potential output to Samsung. So @firstadopter's contrarian read on print night — "SK Hynix missing numbers is **BULLISH** for SK Hynix … a Q2 miss caused by more HBM mix actually means SK Hynix is BETTER positioned in the long run" (2026-07-28) — is directionally supported by the expert work, with the caveat that HBM's 40-60% stable margin is *structurally below* the >80% conventional server DRAM can earn in a spike. **Where the Street landed: KIS (the Korean axe that called the Q2 mechanics correctly) RAISED its target 24% to KRW 4.7m** two days after the print (@jukan05, 2026-07-29), while **BNK cut to KRW 1.48m** (from 1.85m) — an extraordinary 3x spread. Jefferies is BUY at **TP KRW 4,200,000** ("Overblown Fears Present Opportunity"). Price action for the record: **-16.2% on 07-28, -7.6% on 07-29**, ~-20% on the week; **Chairman Chey Tae-won bought 3,620 shares** on 07-30 (@jukan05). Forward pricing to watch: the same expert expects **Q3 DRAM increases to moderate to +13-18% q/q** from Q2's +30%._

Server bits 4.9 → 29.5bn Gb '25→28E (China server share 12% → c.20%); customers ByteDance/Alibaba/Tencent + one domestic-CSP LTA; Q126 OM already 70%. Undersupply until ≥Q2'28 reaffirmed.
