| 08-07 | 🔴 **UBS 2Q26 NDR KEY TAKEAWAYS (Gaudois) — THE BUYBACK QUESTION THE PRINT LEFT OPEN IS NOW DATED BY THE COMPANY: an announcement "WITHIN 3Q26"** | capital / supply / pricing | **bull** | **★ THE CAPITAL-RETURN ITEM, AND IT DIRECTLY RESOLVES THE "no buyback" MISS THIS PAGE LOGGED ON THE 07-28 PRINT: "SK Hynix indicat[ed] that considering ELEVATED FCF GENERATION, IT WOULD NOT WAIT FOR THE END OF THE PERIOD FOR THE CURRENT PROGRAMME (END '27) TO ANNOUNCE INCREMENTAL MEASURES… SK Hynix having disclosed AFTER THE CLOSE ON AUGUST 7TH that they will be MAKING AN ANNOUNCEMENT WITHIN 3Q26. Management is considering BOTH SHARE BUYBACKS AND SPECIAL DIVIDEND (we expect MORE OF A BUYBACK BIAS), and will look at making this SUSTAINABLE LONGER TERM."** **UBS sizes the fuel: FCF of Won186tn/Won306tn/Won357tn in '26E/'27E/'28E, a 2H26 buyback at Won12tn, and over time a move toward returning 50% OF FCF. "For us, AN ANNOUNCEMENT ON SHAREHOLDER RETURN IS THE NO. 1 POSITIVE CATALYST AHEAD."** ⚠️ **Note the disclosure sequencing: the company's own after-close 08-07 statement is the hard fact; the buyback bias, the Won12tn size and the 50%-of-FCF path are all UBS estimates. Consistent with — and independently sourced from — BofA's "50% of FCF, W40tn+ buyback vs W20tn+ dividend" split already logged below, but this is the first time the TIMING comes from the company.** 🔴 **LTAs GET A SCOPE AND A TENOR, which is what the LTA debate on [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) has been missing: "A MEANINGFUL PORTION OF REVENUES WILL BE TIED UP TO LTAs, and >50% OF BITS SOON is a reasonable assumption. LTAs are OVER 5 YEARS, and cover DDR BUT ALSO IN SOME CASES HBM AND NAND."** ⚠️ **">50% of bits under 5-year contracts" is the most consequential structural fact in the note and it cuts both ways — it is exactly the mechanism UBS credits for higher through-cycle ROE and exactly the mechanism Jefferies calls "extenuating the cycle" at softer ASPs. HBM and NAND sitting inside some LTAs is NET-NEW; this wiki had been treating LTAs as a DDR/NAND phenomenon.** **SUPPLY/DEMAND & PRICING: "the market is expected to get EVEN TIGHTER IN 2027, with perhaps DRAM MORE SO THAN NAND… Demand upside continues to materialise for AI/DC across SERVER DDR AND SSD." SK Hynix "may grow DRAM bit shipments OVER 25% IN 2026 (UBSe +26%) but LIKELY AT A LOWER RATE IN 2027 (UBSe: 20%)," and "expects PRICING UP QoQ IN 3Q26/4Q26."** 🔴 **HBM DE-SPEC — MANAGEMENT'S OWN FRAMING, and it is the cleanest company-side answer to the bear this wiki has carried since 08-06: "Whilst NOT COMMENTING on possible de-specing, IF IT WAS TO OCCUR IT WOULD REFLECT SUPPLY SHORTAGE, NOT A CHANGE IN DEMAND." And on the reset: "2027 PRICING RESET IS STILL SEEKING TO BRING [HBM] PROFITABILITY IN LINE WITH DDR."** ⚠️ **That second clause matters for the "HBM-vs-EPS paradox" on this page: management is targeting HBM economics UP toward conventional DRAM, not defending a premium.** **CAPACITY/CAPEX: "Capex will LIKELY GO UP IN 2027 (UBSe: +30% YoY). The pace of YONGIN INFRASTRUCTURE BUILD IS BEING ACCELERATED WITH AN INITIAL FOCUS ON DRAM. M17 CONSTRUCTION STARTED FOR NAND WITH PRODUCTION BY 2029. DALIAN still has some floor space to fill (UBSe +15K WPM CAPACITY IN '27)."** ⚠️ **"Yongin accelerated, DRAM first" is a modest hawkish revision against the ~$38-39bn / June-2029-first-cleanroom timeline logged below — Korean supply is trending faster, not slower, even if none of it lands inside the 2026-28 window. Dalian +15K wpm in '27 IS inside the window and is the only near-term supply item in the note.** **ESTIMATES CUT, RATING AND PT HELD: Buy, PT Won3,000,000 (spot Won1,495,000 at 06-Aug); ADR PT US$210 (was US$204 — FX only). '26-29 OP forecasts lowered ~5% on average on slightly reduced NAND ASPs ("contract pricing negotiations A TOUCH SOFTER in the near term") and '27 HBM bit shipments cut to 21bn Gb (was 23bn), only partly offset by higher HBM ASPs (+64% in 2027, was +61%). FY27 EPS Won547,875 (was Won576,936, −5.0%); FY26 Won390,164 (was Won396,943, −1.7%). UBS is 12% ABOVE '27E CONSENSUS, "which we believe HAS NOW FACTORED IN LTA ADJUSTMENTS" (UBSe '27 OP +11.7% / EPS +14.4% vs cons).** ⚠️ **Valuation marks for the drawdown record: shares DOWN 49% FROM THEIR 22 JUNE PEAK, trading 1.80x NTM P/BV "discounting a LONG-TERM ROE OF 20.5% VS OUR ESTIMATE OF 41.1%"; UBS values the shares at 3.61x NTM P/BV on a 41.1% LT ROE and 11.4% CoE. Scenario band: Won3.30m upside (+121%) / Won3.00m base (+101%) / Won800k downside (−46%).** (UBS Global Research · Nicolas Gaudois / Jimmy Yoon / Luke Yoo, "SK Hynix — 2Q26 NDR Key Takeaways", 2026-08-07) |
| 08-07 | 🔴 **A ~US$38-39BN KOREAN FAB COMMITMENT — and the dates are the point: FIRST CLEANROOM JUNE 2029, WFE INSTALLATION 2031** | supply / capital | **mixed** | **The disclosure: SK Hynix plans a ~$38BN expansion of its Korean chipmaking facilities. "YONGIN Y2 WILL BE THE SECOND OF FOUR FABS PLANNED at the Yongin semiconductor cluster, and the company intends to BREAK GROUND ON THE PROJECT IN JULY NEXT YEAR, TARGETING JUNE 2029 FOR OPENING THE FIRST CLEANROOM." The budget also includes M17 (likely for both DRAM and NAND). DIGITIMES reports the figure as US$39bn across new Yongin and Cheongju fabs.** **JPM's Jay Kwon reads it as IN LINE, not incremental: "consistent with the company's plan to FILL ITS ENTIRE WSPM AT Y1 BY 2030 (NEAR 400K WSPM). THEY NEED TO START INVESTING NOW FOR THE NEXT LEG OF WFE INSTALLATION (AT Y2) IN 2031 — so basically IN LINE WITH OUR EXPECTATIONS."** 🔴 **THE ANALYTICAL POINT IS THE TIMELINE, AND IT CUTS BULLISH FOR THE CYCLE: ground-breaking in July 2027, first cleanroom June 2029, WFE in 2031. NONE OF THIS IS SUPPLY RELIEF FOR THE 2026-28 WINDOW UNDER DEBATE. It is the reason UBS can forecast DRAM undersupply "THROUGH AT LEAST 2Q28" without contradiction, and it corroborates [[LRCX]] CFO Bettinger's constraint framing logged on 08-06 ("greenfield clean room construction takes ~3 YEARS REGARDLESS OF GEOGRAPHY").** ⚠️ **The counterweight, from Jefferies the same day and pointing the other way on NAND: "[[LRCX]]'s strong performance and RECORD NAND REVENUE indicate that 'SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY' in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED. This raises concerns about potential future OVERSUPPLY." A long-dated greenfield commitment and a near-term equipment-led NAND capacity add are not the same thing — both logged, neither netted.** **Capital returns, per BofA: SK Hynix expected to return 50% of FCF "WITH A GREATER EMPHASIS ON BUYBACKS VS CASH DIVIDENDS, SUCH AS W40TN+ VS W20TN+."** **And the 2Q ASP gap this page logged on 08-06 now has its recovery number in BofA's model: DRAM ASP "UP ONLY 30% QoQ VS 45-70% AMONG PEERS" in 2Q, with "A RAPID 3Q ASP RECOVERY OF 25% AND MORE MEANINGFUL HBM4 SHIPMENTS."** (Bloomberg via JPM "Chips for Breakfast" · Joshua Meyers / Jay Kwon, 2026-08-07; DIGITIMES 2026-08-07; BofA Global Memory Tech · Simon Woo, 2026-08-06/07; Jefferies Semis EPS Debrief · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07) |
| 08-04 | **JPM · Jay Kwon — "Major AI Capex Durability Trade beneficiary available at cheap valuations" · OW, ▼PT CUT TO W2,750,000 (from W3,000,000), timeframe extended to Jun-27 (from Dec-26)** | valuation | **mixed** | **★ A PT CUT WITH RAISED EARNINGS — read the mechanism, not the headline, because it is entirely a de-rating of the multiple: "we reset our target multiple to 7x F12M P/E (FROM 8x) and extend our timeframe to Jun-27." Spot W1,564,000. EPS was RAISED 3-16% through FY26E-28E vs the May-26 marks (higher FY26E NP mainly on Kioxia stake-sale impact; FY27E-28E +3-7%), on (i) higher 2H26 DRAM ASP (prev. +10%/+5% for 3Q/4Q), (ii) higher NAND ASP off a stronger 2Q base, (iii) HBM ASP up on the ongoing S-D mismatch, (iv) higher bit production on capacity pull-in + faster migration. FY26E-28E capex raised to W187trn (from W175trn). FY26E-28E EPS CAGR +26%.** ⚠️ **Kwon's own explanation for cutting the multiple while raising numbers is the most useful sentence for this page's durability debate: "the road to a multiple normalization (FTM P/E from <4x to 6x or above) WILL TAKE TIME in the current market environment. Reflecting HIGHER OPPORTUNITY COST vs. the past (i.e. crystal clear capital allocation plan delivery and stronger memory consumption in AI computation thesis required to cast away the negative market narrative IN THE THIRD YEAR OF THE AI CAPEX RACE)."** — i.e. the earnings are not in dispute; the market's willingness to pay for them is, and JPM is now pricing that patience explicitly. **Recommendation stays accumulate: "risk-reward remains favorable on a 12 month horizon and we would take the recent correction as an opportunity to add positions."** 🔴 **THE ANSWER TO THE OPEN LTA QUESTION THIS PAGE FLAGGED ON 08-02, and it is partial: "SKH reported successful LTA with TEN KEY CUSTOMERS (JPMe: OVER HALF OF TOTAL VOLUME largely composed of CSP and AI memory customers) and there appears to be VARIOUS PRICING TERMS (floor price, take-or-pay, prepayment, ceiling price WITH/WITHOUT price hike terms)."** ⚠️ **So SK hynix's book is heterogeneous where [[SAMSUNG]] disclosed a uniform asymmetric collar (sub-5% down vs 10-20%+ up on 60-70% of sales). "Ceiling price with/without price hike terms" means SOME of Hynix's contracts cap the upside — which is the thing Samsung's terms explicitly do not. Contract details are NDA-bound, so this remains the most important undisclosed contractual fact in the complex; it is now better characterised, not resolved.** **On the despec debate, Kwon takes the constructive side and quantifies it: "Key customers' recent decision for content adjustment seems to be OPTIMIZATION INITIATIVES OVER DEMAND CORRECTION (i.e. total HBM procurement demand is UNCHANGED, similar to the SOCAMM content noise). Migration back to 8Hi will ENABLE GREATER OUTPUT to help reduce the S-D gap from 30-40% to a 10-20% gap (SHORTAGE MARKET NATURE INTACT)" — with HBM bit shipments +30%/+55% y/y in FY26E-27E.** ⚠️ **He has been deliberately conservative on HBM pricing and stays there: "we have been advocates of MORE REALISTIC HBM ASP JUMPS next year (<40%) vs bullish expectations (50-100% increase) and pencil in +35% blended HBM ASP increase for FY27E"** — against **HBM OPM above 70% next year, ahead of FY24-25 levels**. **On the 2Q ASP miss: "we take it as a ONE-OFF QUARTER with multiple mix changes and price hike recognition timing issues coming together," expecting server DRAM ASP to move up more than peers in 3Q and blended DRAM ASP above the industry average on HBM4 contribution. 3Q26E/4Q26E OP W78trn/W93trn — "largely in-line with recent consensus."** **NAND: industry-leading mix with eSSD at 70-80% of NAND sales and QLC traction ahead of peers.** **Next catalyst named: "a BUYBACK could demonstrate clear evidence on how management perceives the duration of the up-cycle… JPMe: within the next couple of months"** — consistent with the 08-03 ADR-disclosure-constraint explanation on this page. (JPM · Jay Kwon / Sangsik Lee / Neelay Kamath, 2026-08-04) |
| 08-04 | **BofA · Simon Woo — "SK Hynix appears undervalued vs super-cycle earnings — COVERAGE RESUMED" · Buy, ▼PO W3,000,000 (from W3,590,000); ADR INITIATED at US$250** | valuation | **bull** | **★ Coverage resumed with a LOWER PO but an unchanged bull case — and, like JPM the same day, the cut is a multiple decision: PO W3.0mn on 8x 2027-28E P/E (vs the prior W3.59mn on 9x). Woo's framing of the 8x: "the mid-point between the previous peak cycle (4-5x in the 2017-18 cloud boom) and the long-term historical average (low teens)… slightly below our fair multiples for global DRAM peers of 9-11x."** 🆕 **NEW INSTRUMENT ON THE PAGE: BofA initiates the ADR at PO US$250, "at a premium of 20% to the local shares, reflecting both the post-IPO observed premium and consistent with [[MU]]'s 2027-28E P/E premium above Hynix's local-share multiple."** **Five stated reasons for Buy: (1) long-term strong chip orders from US Big Tech; (2) STABLE ASP in 2027-28 ("no hard landing"); (3) super-cycle-level OP (W300tn+ annual run-rate from 3Q26); (4) dominant position in high-end memory (HBM, LPDDR5, eSSD); (5) P/E of only ~4x on 2027-28E EPS.** **The 2Q26 actuals as BofA states them: sales W79.3tn (+51% q/q, +257% y/y), OP W60.5tn, OPM 76% (DRAM 81% / NAND 65%), NP W93.9tn incl. Kioxia gains (NPM 118%). OP slightly below consensus on a limited DRAM ASP increase (+30% q/q vs its own and consensus 40-50%) "due to some PRE-AGREED QUARTERLY CONTRACT PRICES."** 🔴 **The most consequential claim, and it directly contradicts the standard bear reading of LTAs: "we see a quick resumption of normal contract-price negotiations and more favorable new ASP agreements with US Big Tech customers, EVEN UNDER LTAs (ASP IS NOT REALLY FIXED AND IS MOSTLY NEGOTIABLE EVERY QUARTER, EVEN FOR FIVE-YEAR CONTRACTS)."** ⚠️ **Hold that against JPM's same-day disclosure that some Hynix LTAs carry CEILING PRICES — both cannot be fully true across the whole book. The reconcilable version: LTAs are renegotiated quarterly in practice but a subset contain caps. Left unreconciled and flagged.** **Forecasts: DRAM ASP +25% q/q in 3Q (⚠️ "exceeding even the bullish camp's views — TrendForce's forecast: sub-15%") and +9% in 4Q; HBM4 to 50%+ of total HBM sales in 4Q; quarterly OP W81tn/W90tn in 3Q/4Q26 then W90-100tn per quarter through 2027-28; FCF W100tn+ in 2026 and W200tn+ p.a. in 2027-28 despite W50tn+ p.a. capex; broadly STABLE ASP through 2027 (LSD% q/q moves) then softer 2028 (gradual q/q declines, ~10% y/y fall).** ⚠️ **The 2028 ASP-decline assumption is explained by strategy rather than weakness: management is promoting higher memory content per device (HBM content from 288GB for Rubin to 1,000GB for Rubin Ultra, "or at least 500GB+"), so Hynix "may focus on stable prices/margins while targeting 20%+/- annual bit growth."** **Shipment growth accelerates from 2H27 as Yongin starts, but "the SUFFICIENCY RATIO (Hynix's chip supply vs orders) will likely remain BELOW 100%, implying CONTINUED SHORTAGES."** **Risk dismissals: Samsung capex (HBM share still 30-40% likely), Chinese capacity (actual supply still below 10% of 2028E global TAM), US Big Tech debt financing ("too early to expect AI data-center capex cuts").** (BofA · Simon Woo, 2026-08-04) |
| 08-04 | **@jukan05 · @The_AI_Investor · @ParadisLabs · @trendforce (FinTwit corpus) — HBF standard published, and the 2027 allocation round is already closed** | supply | **bull** | 🆕 **THE HBF SPEC IS OUT AND [[GOOG]] IS IN THE CONSORTIUM — the concrete step this page has been waiting on since the Aug-2025 SanDisk MOU (see Catalysts): "SK hynix Unveils First HBF Standard Specifications with Sandisk, Presenting AI Memory Solutions at FMS 2026" (@firstadopter, 2026-08-04), with @jukan05: "Google has joined the HBF consortium led by SK hynix and Sandisk."** JPM's same-day detail: **Google and Tenstorrent are key ecosystem partners; capacity up to 512GB on a two-stack configuration (8-Hi and 16-Hi dies); bandwidth grades 0.4-3.0TB/s**; part of a full AI-centric NAND line-up extending the SLC-based eSSD work, with **V10 375-layer 4D NAND to be revealed at FMS and mass production targeted early next year.** Roadmap per @ParadisLabs: **SanDisk prototypes 2H26, pilot line complete 2H26, commercialisation targeted 2027, Japan a candidate production site (ETNews); TrendForce sees HBF demand inflecting in 2030.** TrendForce frames it as **complementary to HBM rather than competing** — addressing HBM's cost and capacity limits for LLM inference. ⚠️ **The dissent is specific and worth carrying: @bubbleboi (who is at FMS meeting SK and SanDisk execs) says "my sources tell me that the MAIN CUSTOMER FOR HBF IS CHINESE (Huawei?) which makes sense since they are completely blacklisted from HBM… The success of HBF will hinge on RELIABILITY and my view is it's 2 YEARS AT BEST… my guess is disaggregated memory will be a better solution" (2026-08-04). If the anchor demand is a sanctioned buyer, the addressable market is far smaller than the consortium framing implies. Unverified single source.** 🔴 **SEPARATELY, THE ALLOCATION FACT THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE PRICE PRINTS: "Everyone knows 2027 memory is sold out. That's not the news. The news is THE 2027 ALLOCATION ROUND ALREADY CLOSED, QUIETLY, IN JULY AND AUGUST… Deposits are now universal. Even small buyers who'll never get a 3-5 year LTA are WIRING CASH UPFRONT to hold a slot. VOLUME IS LOCKED, PRICE IS NOT — 2027 DRAM and NAND ASPs get set NEAR SHIPMENT. Anyone assuming LTAs cap the upside HAS IT BACKWARDS: the suppliers kept the optionality. Fill rates running 60-70% of what buyers asked for" (@The_AI_Investor, 2026-08-03).** Corroborated by @jukan05/@aleabitoreddit citing DigiTimes: **2027 DRAM/HBM capacity fully booked ahead of schedule; NAND not as tight but bookings expected filled by end-August; customers allocated only 60-70% of requested volumes; buyers are not publicising their allocations for fear of shrinking them.** ⚠️ **"Volume locked, price not" is the single most important structural claim in this cluster and it sits ON BofA's side of the LTA argument (quarterly negotiability) and AGAINST JPM's ceiling-price disclosure. Third-party/FinTwit sourced — treat as channel colour, not company-confirmed, but note three independent accounts converge on the 60-70% fill rate.** ⚠️ **The one negative datapoint in the cluster, and it is Hynix-specific: Counterpoint's Q2 DRAM share figures show "SK Hynix's revenue-based market share is only about 1 PERCENTAGE POINT AHEAD OF MICRON'S… attributed to HBM3E PRICES ACTUALLY FALLING THROUGH RENEGOTIATIONS rather than rising, along with the impact of DELAYS IN THE LAUNCH OF HBM4. Another factor is that SK Hynix signed its LTAs EARLIER than its competitors, which means Hynix's fixed LTA prices are reportedly LOWER than those of its rivals" (@jukan05, 2026-08-04). That is the first-mover-disadvantage version of the LTA question and it is the cleanest bear datapoint on the page this week.** |

Two large accelerator buyers, opposite signals, on the same day. The honest position: HBM content per GPU is negotiable at the margin, aggregate bit demand still exceeds supply — which is exactly the 10-20% residual gap JPM models.** |
| 08-04 | **Invest Like the Best podcast · Gavin Baker (Atreides) — "what would you do if you were the CEO of Hynix?"** | supply | **bull** | ⚠️ *Informal podcast commentary (~2026-08-04), not verified financials.* **The LTA game theory argued from the buyer's seat, and the conclusion is that the floors hold: four buyers matter at scale ([[AMZN]]/Trainium, [[GOOG]]/TPU, [[AMD]], [[NVDA]] "much bigger than everybody else combined"), memory-per-flop is "the single most important thing you could do to increase token output per unit of compute" ("the axis that is dominating all others" — no demand elasticity so far), and accelerator market shares "for the next several years are going to be determined by supply-chain allocations and what you have pre-purchased." Therefore: "if you break your LTA and in the next two or three years FOR ANY REASON leverage shifts back to the memory guys, YOU'RE OUT OF BUSINESS… they break the volume agreement and give the volume to your competitor" — and "the game theory almost even holds in a severe oversupply situation." The old-cycle contrast is [[AAPL]]-as-monopsonist: "even if they super screw Hynix, Micron will of course take them… This is just different."** [The direct buy-side rebuttal to the "buyers describe the option to stop paying" thread on [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).] **Asked directly what he'd do as CEO of SK hynix: "I'd do the exact same thing Nvidia is doing right now… I would be going to the buyers of GPUs, Trainiums, and whoever, and saying I'll PARTICIPATE IN THE NVIDIA CREDIT WRAPPER" — put up some cash from today's cash flow into the financing structure and take "a cut of the ongoing revenues," i.e. a ROYALTY ON RECURRING COMPUTE REVENUE, "a logical extension of the LTAs where they're trading upside for durability… I'm sure our friends at Blackstone and Apollo are suggesting some variant of this to the memory companies."** A structural-capital-allocation idea to watch against the SCA/prepayment structures already on this page — the direction of travel he describes (memory suppliers converting spot upside into contracted, financeable, royalty-like streams) is the same one the 16-agreement SCA disclosure started. (Invest Like the Best · Gavin Baker, Atreides, ~2026-08-04) [Source](../_equity_calls/Overall/2026-08-04_GavinBaker_ILTB-AI-repricing.md) |
| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update" — THE PRIMARY CALL behind the relay row below, and read directly it is a WIN on the item that matters** | supply | **bull** | ★ **THE VOLUME SENTENCE, VERBATIM: "from the Rubin HBM side, in the supply chain, we currently only see a SMALL VOLUME OF MICRON AND SAMSUNG'S HBM. And the RUBIN WITH HYNIX HBM4 WILL START TO RAMP UP AT LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME, WHICH IS WHY IN Q4 WE WILL SEE A HIGHER VOLUME OF RUBIN."** ⚠️ **The causal direction is what upgrades this from sequencing trivia to a franchise datapoint: Fubon is not saying Hynix is late *within* the Rubin ramp — he is saying THE RUBIN RAMP IS BACK-HALF WEIGHTED BECAUSE HYNIX'S HBM4 ARRIVES THEN. Rubin production of 200-300K units in Q3 steps to 700-800K in Q4 on that arrival. So the "Hynix behind Samsung and Micron" headline (relay row below) and this are the same fact seen from two ends: the peers are qualified first at token volume; SK hynix is the vendor the platform's volume curve is built around. On this page's own terms that is the moat argument intact, not impaired — and it re-dates the Q2 "HBM4 ramp delay" revenue miss as a LATE-Q3/Q4 revenue event rather than a lost socket.** _(machine transcript; ASR garbles names — `Fubon`←"Foobon", `Blackwell`←"Black Wheel"/"Blackwall", `CoWoS`←"CoOS".)_ **The rest of the memory slice, all Fubon: HBM4 cost to NVDA at $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class — ~1.8x — ⚠ dated "in 2026" in the presentation and "in 2027" in Q&A, ambiguity left standing; a Rubin GPU at $78-80K on an assumed 75-80% NVDA chip GM; Rubin rack BOM $7-8m vs $3-3.5m GB200-class. Content: the four-compute-die Rubin Ultra heard at ">1,024 GB/chip" is CANCELLED and the surviving two-die part is "around 384 GB" (B200 ~192GB, B300 ~288GB) — spec unchanged, shipping SKU lighter. And the ODM rebuttal of the memory-downsizing rumour, in the buyers' words: a module cut "was discussion" but is "unfeasible for AI server," with NO 2027 spec change seen "including the Google, AWS," and purchasing teams "rising the capex for buy and sell for the GPU."** ⚠️ **One item on the debit side that belongs to the whole complex, not to Hynix alone: "the thermal design change AND THE HBM BASE DIE ISSUE" are the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out — an HBM execution problem is now inside the industry's most consequential 2H26 slip. Hynix is the volume HBM4 vendor into that platform, so it owns a share of that risk as well as the upside.** See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) · [MU.md](MU.md) · [SAMSUNG.md](SAMSUNG.md). | 07-30 | Bernstein · David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang ("ASML: Best Idea 3Q26") | produto | **bull** | **Read-through only — an [[ASML]] Best-Idea note with no SK hynix rating or PT. The relevance is that Bernstein names SK hynix as the industry's LEAD High-NA adopter, ahead of every logic player except Intel, and argues DRAM — not logic — is where High-NA economics work best.** Verbatim: **"SK hynix has the clearest early signal: the company announced in September 2025 that it had assembled ASML's TWINSCAN EXE:5200B, the first production-oriented HNA system, at its M16 fab for next-generation DRAM development."** The economic argument behind that lead is specific and structural: **"the strongest economic case for HNA is in DRAM, as DRAM dies are smaller than logic dies and therefore do not require stitching. This translates into higher throughput and a lower cost per exposure, making HNA adoption easier to justify economically."** Bernstein's adoption roadmap (Exhibit 10) has **DRAM HNA beginning with SK hynix and [[SAMSUNG]] at the 1d node**, while [[TSM]] waits until ~A10 at the end of the decade. **The node-migration arithmetic underneath is the part to hold, because it is a capex-intensity claim, not a tool-vendor claim: DRAM EUV exposures per node run ~2 at 1a → 3 at 1b → 5 at 1c → 8 at 1d, rising into the low teens at 0b/0c; DRAM litho intensity moves from <20% at 1Y/1Z → 26% at 1c → ~30% at 1d; and total DRAM EUV exposures compound 4.5x from 3.4 MWPM (2025) to 26.3 MWPM (2030).** ⚠️ **Read honestly, that is a rising cost-per-bit-of-capacity headwind for SK hynix as a litho BUYER, at the same time as it is a demand tailwind for ASML** — the note is written from the vendor's side and does not cost it from the memory maker's P&L. The offset Bernstein offers is process simplification: HNA single-exposure replaces LNA multi-patterning, eliminating masks, litho-etch loops and cut steps, with **litho capex/opex ~1.2x but non-litho cost declining**, so total process cost can fall even as the tool bill rises. Also relevant to the 4F² debate: Bernstein frames the 6F²→4F² transition around 0a as **"a temporary easing of scaling requirements rather than a departure from the long-term trend"** of shrinking features — i.e. it does not read 4F² as a break in the EUV-layer trajectory. Demand context: ASML guided FY26 memory system sales **+75% y/y vs +25% for advanced logic**, with memory now ~50% of system sales (from ~30% historically) — management's word for the DRAM setup was a **"perfect storm"** of node migration and volume capacity adds at once. (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_258544.html) |
| 07-31 | **BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya — Global Memory Tech weekly** | supply | **bull** | **★ The LTA mechanics, and they invert the common bear reading of high contract coverage. "LTA does not fix memory chip price… lot of room to raise contract price every quarter but there is LIMIT FOR PRICE CUT." Specifically: Samsung said 60-70% OF ITS MEMORY SALES WILL BE UNDER LTA, but with ASYMMETRIC terms — a price-cut FLOOR of sub-5% down q/q against price INCREASES broadly open at 10-20%+. And on tenor: Samsung's LTAs (mainly for US Big Tech) are mostly 5-YEAR ROLLING, so a 6th year can be added right after or before the 1st year completes.** ⚠️ **So LTAs here are not price locks — they are DOWNSIDE COLLARS WITH UNCAPPED UPSIDE on a rolling tenor. High LTA coverage is therefore bullish for realised ASP stability rather than a cap on it, which is the opposite of how rising contract coverage is usually read at this point in a cycle.** **Spot: sentiment positive into August on three drivers — (1) chip order increases for inventory restocking; (2) many Tier-2 OEMs resuming smartphone/PC launches for Sept/4Q DESPITE high memory cost; (3) China domestic smartphone shipments bottoming out — only 17MN UNITS IN JUNE vs a normal 25-30mn, with lean channel inventories. ⚠️ Note the DRAM/NAND divergence in the confirmed July contract prints: TrendForce July DRAM contract price UP LOW-TEENS % M/M = 30-50% Q/Q, but FLAT FOR NAND.** **On China supply, the reason it is not showing up in spot: "minimal impact from China local chipmakers' capacity expansion — China-branded memory NOT COMMONLY TRADED IN THE SPOT MARKET; even Korea/US/Japan chips not much available for white-box OEMs."** Adjacent confirmation that the AI-server pull is broad: **SEMCO and LG Innotek both guided bullishly on AI MLCC and FC-BGA, with 2Q OP more than doubling y/y, stronger LTA orders, LARGE PREPAYMENTS funding new fab construction, and contract price increases through 2H and 2027 — and all three top MLCC makers (SEMCO, Murata, Taiyo Yuden) have raised prices.** |

And we have Samsung on Thursday" — so read every figure here as an expectation, not a result.* **The bogey: "for Hynix the numbers were anywhere between 60 to 65 trillion. Our number is currently at 65 trillion. We've seen some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** Against the actual **₩60.54tn OP**, the print landed **at the bottom of the buyside range and ~7% below MS's own ₩65tn** — the drift Sean flagged was directionally right, and the houses that had not cut were the ones marked. **His framing is the more valuable part: "the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** What the tape then delivered — SK Hynix **opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%** while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours — reads as an **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive** result on Sean's own test for the name and a clearly negative one for the complex. **On the buyback: "For Hynix, my view is that it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that"** — against a stated buyside view that "valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory **with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst**." It did not come; that catalyst is now a miss, not a pending item. **The net-new analytical point is on supply: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year and ~24% next** (alongside [[SAMSUNG]]'s P4 second-floor cleanroom) — i.e. the same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is *adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry*. **On CXMT he puts the re-rating in transfer terms: the debut closed at a US$492bn market cap, "the 25th most valuable company in the world upon the IPO. You can probably say that that's quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix"** — while bounding the fundamentals: "probably doesn't impact this cycle, maybe in the outer years," with CXMT "**currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity**" running DDR5 at "slightly lower on the clock speed versus mainstream DRAM, but nevertheless good enough for some of the mid to low end products in China," and the real concern being "**the expansion into 2030 could be more aggressive**." ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag: the "about 30,000 wafers capacity" figure is irreconcilable with the ~290-350k wspm CXMT capacity this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12) — treat it as a transcription or sub-segment artifact and use the WF/BofA numbers.** Sector frame: his three-prong debate (AI spend robust; LTA re-rating still an *upside* story; the cycle) resolves by elimination onto the cycle — **y/y DRAM pricing peaks in 3Q, 3Q hikes ~15-20%, and 4Q would need +60-70% sequential to sustain the second derivative, "a bit tough to achieve."** Also: TrendForce inventory up since 2Q, **first time since 4Q last year, and at module makers not producers**, concentrated in non-AI NAND. (Morgan Stanley webcast "2Q26 memory, Intel implications and ARM preview" · Sean, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_2q26_ms_memory_intel_implications_and_arm_preview.html) |

Everything already carried in the 07-30 row (the ~40%-vs-≤25% HBM capacity mix, +30% vs +mid-40% DRAM ASP, 72%→76% OP margin, HBM4 yield 80-85% vs ~60%, HBM3E $300-400 → HBM4 $500-600, HBM 40-60% margin vs >80% conventional, +13-18% q/q 3Q DRAM, LTAs, hybrid bonding/3D DRAM, China blocked by export controls) is NOT repeated.** 🔴 **(1) THE SPOT-VS-CONTRACT GAP, QUANTIFIED — this is the cleanest answer on the page to "why did the reported ASP look so weak against the trackers": SK Hynix reported ~+30% DRAM and ~+50% NAND for 2Q, while "TRENDFORCE EXPECTED SECOND QUARTER DRAM INCREASED LIKE 55% AND NAND TO 70%" — "but you have to understand that those numbers are more for the SPOT MARKET prices increases. Not… the CONTRACT prices. The actual price that these big players apply with customers, it is they do not follow the same pattern."** ⚠️ **Anyone marking Korean memory to TrendForce spot is mismeasuring by ~25pts on DRAM and ~20pts on NAND in a spike quarter.** 🔴 **(2) THE INDUSTRY CAPACITY MAP, ON A WAFER-INPUT BASIS — net-new as a cross-company comparison: SAMSUNG ~700K WPM · SK HYNIX ~600K WPM · [[MU]] "maybe half… at most like 300k." DRAM share framing: Samsung "around 35 or 38%," SK Hynix "like 28%." Capacity, not technology, is what he says separates them: "in capacity wise, Samsung provide high volume… each strengths comes from the volume, how much they can produce."** 🔴 **(3) THE PRICE CALL THAT CUTS AGAINST THE HBM BULLS, and it is the most contrarian thing on the call: NVIDIA "adjusted 20% increase" on HBM in the first quarter; on the widely-discussed doubling for next year ("HBM4 plus currently around 550, will be doubled next year, like 1,200 or 1,400"), he pushes back — "I SUSPECT THAT NVIDIA WILL INCREASE PRICE LIKE DOUBLE NEXT YEAR BECAUSE NVIDIA… KEEP LIKE 75% THEIR PROFIT [margin on the overall GPU system]. So they do not increase the BOM, the component cost, the HBM cost very much… 100% increase will be MAXIMUM, I think… between 20 to 30% they adjust it."** ⚠️ **Read against the page's other 2027 HBM inputs: Redburn/UBS-style <40%, house-conservative +35% blended (Susquehanna), "50-100%" bulls, and SemiAnalysis's "significant increase… to match commodity-DRAM margins." The expert's +20-30% is the LOW END of the range and the mechanism he gives is [[NVDA]]'s own gross-margin defence — i.e. the customer's margin policy as the ceiling on HBM pricing. Logged as an expert opinion, not a forecast.** **(4) THE SUPPLY-SIDE MARKET, STATED AS A NUMBER: "supply only meet like a 60% to 70%" of demand, therefore "they do not differentiate from these companies. WHATEVER THEY PRODUCE, THEY BUY. That's the current situation. This is a SUPPLY SIDE MARKET. But once supply is big enough compared to demand, then it will be different. They will choose better product or cheaper price."** **(5) WHY HE THINKS 2028 IS THE HINGE AND WHY HE STILL DOESN'T FEAR IT: the three players' new fabs land "around this 28 time period," so 2028 is when the balance is tested — but hyperscaler capex is effectively pre-committed ("they PREPAID for some power company or some cooling system… so they cannot reduce now," with the construction realised around 2028), and LTA coverage (Samsung 60-70% of wafer capacity ≈ 80% of revenue) plus CUSTOMISED product means "over supply situation will not be easy. It's not like a prior cycle." Price increases "will be moderated after 28, but still it will grow."** **(6) 3D DRAM commercialisation targeted "maybe in 2032 or 2033," because below the 1D node "it is practically difficult to scale down further"; hybrid bonding replaces TCB from HBM4E/HBM5 for speed, energy efficiency and heat dissipation.** (BTG Pactual-hosted expert call · Hongkyo Jung, former SK Hynix executive, "Beyond the Earnings: What SK Hynix & Samsung Tell Us About the Memory Cycle", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_btg_former_hynix_expert_30_07_26.html) |
| 07-30 | BTG Pactual · Hongkyo Jung (former SK Hynix executive) | produto | bull | **Expert call, "Beyond the Earnings: What SK Hynix & Samsung Tell Us About the Memory Cycle." This is the cleanest explanation on the page for why SK hynix's headline DRAM ASP looked weak.** "SK Hynix's Q2 DRAM price increase (**30%**) was lower than Samsung's (**mid-40%**) because Hynix has a significantly higher proportion of its capacity (**~40%**) dedicated to HBM, whose prices are set further in advance and do not follow volatile spot market trends as much as conventional DRAM" — Samsung allocates a **maximum of 25%** to HBM, so more of its capacity captured the conventional DRAM spike. **Margins: SK hynix Q2 operating margin 76%, up from 72%** — "exceeding margins seen in companies like TSMC or Apple." **HBM yields are the moat: SK hynix HBM4 yield ~80-85% ("matured," similar to prior generations), vs Samsung ~60%** — the yield gap "significantly impacts profitability (losing 40% of potential product)" and is what lets SK hynix supply Nvidia's Rubin volumes. Pricing ladder: **HBM3E $300-400 → HBM4 $500-600** on double the I/Os. Margin structure: **HBM 40-60% stable, vs conventional advanced server DRAM sometimes >80% during sharp price spikes** — i.e. HBM mix is a near-term drag and a long-term moat. Expects **Q3 DRAM price increases to moderate to +13-18% q/q**. On the 2028 oversupply risk: mitigated by LTAs — **Samsung projects 60-70% of wafer capacity (≈80% of revenue) LTA-driven**. Next-gen: **hybrid bonding** and **3D DRAM** to overcome scaling limits. On China: "Chinese memory makers are effectively **blocked from competing in advanced DRAM/HBM due to US export controls on critical manufacturing equipment**, cementing the market dominance of the 'big three'" |
| 07-30 | Jefferies · Jeff (via SILK ROAD) | valuation | bull | **BUY, TP KRW 4,200,000 — "Overblown Fears Present Opportunity."** Argues the recent selloff is extreme and creates an opportunity given where the stock now trades. (Context on the move: SK hynix fell **-16.2% on 07-28 and a further -7.6% on 07-29**, ~-20% on the week, after the 2Q miss — @zerohedge, 2026-07-29) |
| 07-30 | Morgan Stanley · (Korea semis team) | demanda | neutral | Hosted **"SK Hynix/Samsung 2Q26 Post-Result Takeaways"** webinar, 2026-07-30 08:00 São Paulo — flagged here as the post-print buy-side touchpoint |
| 07-30 | DIGITIMES | supply | bull | "**South Korean suppliers win HBM4 tester orders from SK Hynix**" — supply-chain confirmation of the HBM4 ramp SK hynix guided to for 2H26 |

**Síntese do quarter:** the first headline miss of the cycle — **OP ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn, revenue ₩79.3tn vs ₩83.9tn** — and the most important qualifier is that it is a miss against a **stale screen consensus**: local brokers were already at ~₩60tn and Jefferies' analyst at ₩62tn. Margins did not break (**GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA 81%**) and the sequential level still rose off Q1. **The genuine negative is mechanical and specific: the revenue shortfall is attributed to the HBM4 ramp being late**, even though HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 and 1cnm HBM4E samples are already with a major customer. Two things make this print more consequential than its numbers. **(1) The moat is being re-read as a constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to feed the view that **SK Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing precisely because of its HBM commitments to [[NVDA]]** — the anchor relationship that underwrites this page's supplier-differentiation thesis is now the reason it may be leaving price on the table. **(2) An ~80% capex increase to the high-₩40tn range, with no buyback despite large net cash**, lands in the same week Jefferies research calls **memory prices to peak ~1H27** with hikes already tracking **15-20% vs 25-30% expected** and **2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex**. That is the bear case in one sentence: peak-margin cash being committed to a capacity step-up into a price peak. Set against it, management's demand framing got *broader*, not narrower — **agentic AI expanding demand across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD**, PC/mobile softness explicitly a **volume-allocation** problem rather than end-demand — and DRAM ASP was still **+30% q/q**. The unresolved question for the next two quarters is whether **HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls**, with **CXMT's +466% IPO debut** now supplying a permanent sentiment overhang on the China supply question. **Two additions from MS's pre-print webcast (Sean, 07-28) that change how the print should be scored.** First, the **bogey**: MS was at **₩65tn** with the buyside range **₩60-65tn** and "adjustment towards the 60 handle" already underway — so the **₩60.54tn** print landed at the *bottom* of the range and ~7% below MS, which makes the "stale screen consensus" defence above accurate but narrower than it reads: the sell-side had cut, MS had not. Sean's own test was explicit and better than the number — **"the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end"** — and the reaction (**Hynix opening -6% and recovering to -2.3%**, while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours) scores as **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name and negative for the complex**, not the clean bounce he was looking for. Second, and analytically the more useful: **the HBM4 delay is not only a revenue event, it is a supply event.** Sean names **"Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result"** as a driver of TrendForce industry **DRAM bit growth moving 25% → ~31% this year (~24% next)** — so the freed wafer lands in commodity DRAM, where returns are currently competitive with HBM per this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox. Hold all three legs: **share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental industry supply feeding exactly the cycle-durability bear the Jefferies note is pressing.** Finally, the **buyback that did not come was an expected catalyst, not an absent question** — Sean, pre-print: "it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility," against a buyside "of the view that valuations are clearly an attractive entry point for memory with the potential buyback as near-term catalyst." Contrast [[SAMSUNG]], where the same webcast argues the buyback window is structurally *year-end* rather than simply absent. See [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md). **Update 2026-08-03 (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur, hosted by Jefferies — the primary supply-chain call): the first leg of the unresolved question now has an answer, and it is the favourable one. The question this Síntese posed was whether "HBM4 catches up before pricing rolls." Fubon dates the catch-up: "Rubin with Hynix HBM4 will start to ramp up at LATE AUGUST OR SEPTEMBER WITH A BIGGER VOLUME, which is why in Q4 we will see a higher volume of Rubin" — with only "a small volume of Micron and Samsung's HBM" in the chain so far. Rubin production of 200-300K units in Q3 stepping to 700-800K in Q4 is therefore PACED BY SK HYNIX, which reframes the "moat as a constraint" reading: the NVDA anchor relationship is what the platform's volume curve is built on, and the Q2 miss reads as timing inside a socket the company still owns rather than share loss to the peers. Against it, three offsets to keep live: (i) the HBM4 $/GB step-up to $30-32/GB from $17-18/GB HBM3-class is being captured across all three suppliers, so it is a cycle fact rather than a Hynix edge (⚠ the transcript dates it "in 2026" in one place and "in 2027" in another; ambiguity carried); (ii) the surviving Rubin Ultra SKU is LIGHTER on HBM — the four-compute-die ">1,024 GB/chip" part is cancelled and the two-die part is "around 384 GB" (B200 ~192GB / B300 ~288GB) — so content per package is a genuine headwind even though ODMs reject any 2027 memory de-spec "including the Google, AWS" and are raising purchasing capex against a $7-8m Rubin rack; (iii) "the HBM base die issue" is one of the two named causes of the ~two-month Rubin push-out, so the volume vendor also carries the execution risk. Net: the ramp-timing leg of the bear case weakens materially from late August; the pricing-peak and content-per-package legs are untouched.**

**🆕 08-09/10 — the bear-headline week, and what actually changed:** three of the four drags (HBM discount, Solidigm IPO, Chongqing stake) are **headline risk that management has now put on a ≤1-month disclosure clock**, not confirmed facts; the fourth (W54tn capex) is confirmed but back-end loaded to 2031. The genuinely new bear datapoint is competitive rather than corporate: **Samsung HBM4 yield "nears 80%"** (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10) — i.e. the second source is qualifying, into a 2027 HBM price negotiation SKH is already being reported as discounting. Against that, **JPM prefers SEC > SKH in the near term** inside an OW-on-both memory call (JPM / Kwon, 2026-08-09), and **SIG prefers Micron specifically on "HBM share gains against SK Hynix"** (SIG / Hosseini, 2026-08-10). Two of the three houses that touched Korean memory this week are routing incremental money *away* from SKH within a bullish memory view — that is the read, not the tape. `demanda/valuation · mixed`

| DRAM | 128GB+ DDR5 shipments >2x QoQ; "sold out 2026" | 1cnm full mass production; 256GB DDR5 RDIMM | "Significant price increases"; customers prioritize volume | Q2 bits +HSD, **ASP +30% q/q**; 2026 bit growth **mid-20s%**; Q3 bits **+10%**, server-focused; SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C |

| Demand / supply balance | 2026 contracts finalized; tightness into 2027 | Could not fully meet HBM demand; 2026 locked | Tight supply; flags PC/mobile softening on high prices | Demand **broadening as AI turns agentic**; Big Tech expanding procurement on a **compute-capacity shortage**; **PC/mobile softness = difficulty securing volumes**, recovery as tightness eases |

  🔴 **THE ~US$38-39BN KOREAN FAB COMMITMENT, AND THE DATES ARE THE FINDING: "YONGIN Y2 WILL BE THE SECOND OF FOUR FABS planned at the Yongin cluster… BREAK GROUND IN JULY NEXT YEAR, TARGETING JUNE 2029 FOR OPENING THE FIRST CLEANROOM," budget also covering M17 (DRAM and NAND). JPM's Jay Kwon calls it IN LINE, not incremental: "consistent with the plan to FILL ITS ENTIRE WSPM AT Y1 BY 2030 (NEAR 400K WSPM). They need to start investing now for the NEXT LEG OF WFE INSTALLATION (AT Y2) IN 2031."** ✅ **NONE OF THIS IS SUPPLY RELIEF FOR THE 2026-28 WINDOW UNDER DEBATE — which is precisely why UBS can carry DRAM undersupply "through at least 2Q28" without contradiction, and it corroborates [[LRCX]] CFO Bettinger's "greenfield clean room construction takes ~3 YEARS REGARDLESS OF GEOGRAPHY" logged 08-06.**
  ⚠️ **THE COUNTERWEIGHT, SAME DAY, OPPOSITE SIGN, ON NAND: Jefferies — "[[LRCX]]'s strong performance and RECORD NAND REVENUE indicate that 'SOMEONE'S ADDING CAPACITY' in NAND, LIKELY THE KOREANS, EARLIER THAN PREVIOUSLY ANTICIPATED. BOTH LOGGED, NOT NETTED — the equipment layer is the leading indicator here, and it is now the one to watch.**
  **Capital returns sized by BofA: 50% of FCF with "a GREATER EMPHASIS ON BUYBACKS VS CASH DIVIDENDS, SUCH AS W40TN+ VS W20TN+" (vs [[SAMSUNG]]'s W30tn+ special dividend / W40tn+ buyback path).** ✅ **And the 2Q ASP gap logged on 08-06 (DRAM ASP "up ONLY 30% q/q vs 45-70% among peers") now has its snap-back number in BofA's industry model: "A RAPID 3Q ASP RECOVERY OF 25% AND MORE MEANINGFUL HBM4 SHIPMENTS" — the figure that settles mix-timing versus competitive position.** ⚠️ **Also relevant: UBS's Rubin Ultra de-spec finding (8-Hi HBM4 → HBM4E, not 12-Hi) cuts per-GPU content but RAISES 2027 [[NVDA]] HBM consumption on higher unit counts — see [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).** Cross-filed [[SAMSUNG]], [[MU]], [[LRCX]], [[NVDA]], [[KIOXIA]], [hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md). - **2026-08-06 — /wiki-ingest (scheduled). Nothing superseded; three net-new items, one of which quantifies the 2Q ASP gap that drove the miss.**
  🔴 **BofA RESUMED COVERAGE AND ITS MODEL PUTS A NUMBER ON THE 2Q ASP SHORTFALL: "our industry model now reflects (1) HYNIX'S LOWER 2Q DRAM ASP, UP ONLY 30% QoQ VS 45–70% AMONG PEERS, but a RAPID 3Q ASP RECOVERY OF 25% AND MORE MEANINGFUL HBM4 SHIPMENTS" (BofA · Simon Woo / Dai Shen / Vivek Arya / Mikio Hirakawa / Matt Shin, Global Memory Tech weekly, 2026-08-06).** ⚠️ **This is the cleanest quantification yet of what went wrong in the 2Q miss this page already carries: not volume, not cost — a blended DRAM ASP up 30% q/q against peers at 45–70%, i.e. a 15–40 point ASP gap, consistent with the higher-HBM-mix/LTA-smoothing explanation. And the forward call is a snap-back: +25% q/q in 3Q with more meaningful HBM4. That makes 3Q ASP the single number that settles whether the 2Q miss was mix-timing or a competitive position.**
  **INDUSTRY FRAME from the same model: 4Q26 last-minute hikes of DRAM +8% / NAND +3% q/q, then stable-to-mildly-correcting 2027 with the year-average ASP STILL ABOVE 2026, then a 2028 correction of DRAM −8% / NAND −14% — "no hard-landing, just a healthy correction warranting a high-margin profile." Global DRAM industry sales $134bn/$573bn/$847bn/$917bn in 2025/26/27/28. NAND spot rallied (512Gb–1Tb +5–6%, legacy 256Gb +10%+) on Tier-2/white-box rush orders; DRAM spot +1–2%, "better than seasonal."**
  🔴 **CAPITAL RETURNS ARE NOW THE INVESTOR PRESSURE POINT, and this page already flagged the absence: "Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix face GROWING CALLS FROM INVESTORS wanting a greater share of excess cash via DIVIDENDS OR BUYBACKS, after the pair provided SCANT DETAIL ON CAPITAL RETURNS when reporting AI-driven record profit" (@ReutersBiz, 2026-08-06).** ⚠️ **Directly relevant to the FY capex-up-to-+80%-with-no-buyback decision logged at the 2Q print — the market is now pricing that choice, not just noting it.**
  ⚠️ **THE ADR-vs-KOSPI WEDGE, which invalidates naive [[MU]] comparisons: @dylan522p — "People ask me why Micron trades at a multiple to SK Hynix now that the ADR exists. IT'S VERY SIMPLE. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE EXISTS." @MF_Camillus's rebuttal is the more useful half: "The valuation gap between $MU and $SKHY (ADR) is NEGLIGIBLE, largely because THE ADR ITSELF TRADES AT A SUBSTANTIAL PREMIUM TO THE KOSPI-LISTED SK HYNIX SHARES. Micron's premium would be justified not only by corporate governance: INDEX ELIGIBILITY, INVESTOR ACCESSIBILITY, GEOPOLITICAL ALIGNMENT AND CAPITAL RETURN PROSPECTS matter just as much." FinTwit, no numbers — logged because the ADR/KOSPI premium means any MU-vs-SKHYNIX multiple comparison must specify WHICH line is being compared.**
  **Also: Samsung and SK Hynix are "weighing China-based AMEC's chipmaking equipment for use in their Chinese factories" (Reuters via UBS TECH desk, 08-05) — China-indigenisation datapoint, read-through to [[AMAT]]/[[LRCX]]/[[KLAC]]. And on HBF, [[SAMSUNG]] unveiled zNAND-O at FMS taking High Bandwidth Flash to EDGE AI with Qualcomm and Google evaluating (@trendforce, 08-06) — a competitive/fragmentation item for the HBF standard SK Hynix is co-developing with [[SNDK]].** Sources: BofA · Simon Woo et al. 2026-08-06 · @ReutersBiz, @dylan522p, @MF_Camillus, @trendforce 2026-08-06 · UBS TECH desk 2026-08-05. No rating, PT or estimate superseded.** **Three things this adds to the Q2 print already on the page.** **(1) The bogey, which sharpens the "miss against a stale consensus" defence into something more precise: MS's Sean had the buyside range at ₩60-65tn with "our number… currently at 65 trillion" and "some adjustment towards the 60 handle."** So the **₩60.54tn** actual landed at the *bottom* of the range and **~7% below MS's own number** — the sell-side had cut, MS had not, and the screen consensus of ₩64.2tn was the lagging figure. **(2) His stated test, which is the better scoring rule and worth keeping as a template: "the number itself will not be that meaningful. The reaction to any number would actually give us a better sense of whether this selloff is actually at the tail end. If tomorrow we do have numbers and stocks bouncing, I think that would be good news for the sector."** Scored against the tape — **Hynix opened -6% and recovered to -2.3% while [[MU]] fell ~8.9% intraday plus ~4% after hours** — the result is **ambiguous-to-mildly-constructive for the name, negative for the complex**; not the clean bounce, not the rout. **(3) The genuinely net-new analytical point, and it inverts the sign on the page's central negative: Sean names "Hynix probably HBM3E, where no HBM4 will actually reduce the amount of total HBM and more DRAM will be produced as a result" as one driver of TrendForce industry DRAM bit growth moving from "a 25% type of grow to now reaching 31%" this year (~24% next).** The same HBM4 slip that caused the revenue miss is **adding commodity DRAM bits to the industry** at a moment when commodity-DRAM returns are competitive with HBM (this page's own HBM-vs-EPS paradox, MS/Sean Kim 04-14). Folded into the HBM4 Sinal row as a three-legged read — share/mix negative on the [[NVDA]] franchise, near-term bit/EPS cushion, and incremental supply feeding the cycle-durability bear — rather than logged as a separate positive. Also added: **the buyback was an expected catalyst that did not arrive** ("it could happen, given the current volatility, but again, we have no visibility on that," against a buyside pricing "the potential buyback as near-term catalyst"), now marked as a resolved miss in the Capex/capital-return Sinal row; and Sean's **CXMT-as-transfer framing** — a **US$492bn** debut close, 25th most valuable company globally, "quite proportionate to the amount of market cap that was taken away from stocks like Hynix," with fundamental impact bounded to the outer years and **the 2030 expansion pace as the real question**. ⚠️ **Data-integrity flag raised, not adopted: the webcast's "CXMT currently at about 30,000 wafers capacity" cannot be reconciled with the ~290-350k wspm figures this page and [[MU]] carry from Wells Fargo (07-14) and BofA (07-12); treated as a transcription/sub-segment artifact.** ⚠️ **Date tension noted for the record: the webcast is filed 2026-07-28 but is internally pre-print ("earnings for Hynix tomorrow… Samsung on Thursday" = 07-30), while this page dates the Q2 print 2026-07-28.** The commentary is unambiguously pre-print and has been labelled as such throughout; the one-day filing discrepancy does not affect any figure. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SNDK]].
- **2026-07-28** — **/wiki-ingest · Q2 2026 PRINT (2026-07-28) — new-print reset.** New `## Current state` for the June quarter (the Q1 2026 block plus all the standing capacity/HBM4/MR-MUF/packaging deep-dive material is retained inline below it); archived the Q1 2026 intra-quarter window (Apr 23 → Jul 28, **130 lines** — the largest window on the wiki) into this Changelog; opened a fresh Q2 2026 window; rolled the management-evolution table forward (Q2 2025 drops off, **Q2 2026 added**). **Superseded per the thesis-drift rule** — prior values preserved: *Current state* previously led with **Q1 2026 revenue ₩52.6tn (+60% q/q, +198% y/y), operating profit ₩37.6tn, OP margin 72%, net profit ₩40.3tn, EBITDA ₩41.3tn, net cash ₩35tn.** **What printed: revenue ₩79.3tn vs ~₩83.9tn expected and operating profit ₩60.54tn vs ₩64.2tn consensus — the first headline miss of the cycle.** ⚠️ **The qualifier matters as much as the miss: local Korean brokers had already cut OP to ~₩60tn and Jefferies' Jeff Kim was at ₩62tn**, so the print is in line with the most recent numbers and only misses the stale screen consensus. Margin structure intact — **GM 83%, OM 76%, EBITDA margin 81%** — and the *level* still rose sequentially off Q1. **The margin trajectory this page has tracked (41% → 47% → 58% → 72%) therefore breaks its ascent at 76%.** **Cause of the shortfall, per Jefferies: the HBM4 ramp is late** — HBM4 shipments did begin in Q2 at customer-required speeds and **1cnm HBM4E samples went to a major customer in 1H**, but the ramp slipped relative to plan. Other management detail logged: **DRAM Q2 bits +HSD with ASP +30% q/q, 2026 bit growth guided mid-20s%, Q3 bits +10% server-focused**; **SOCAMM2 fully commercialised on 1C**; demand **broadening as AI evolves into agentic form** across HBM/server DRAM/eSSD; **PC and smartphone softness explicitly attributed to difficulty securing memory volumes, not weak end-demand.** **Two developments change the shape of the debate rather than the numbers.** **(1) The [[NVDA]] anchor is being re-read as a pricing constraint.** Jefferies expects the miss to fuel the view that **"Hynix has been less aggressive than [[SAMSUNG]] on pricing given its commitments to NVDA on HBM"** — i.e. the supplier-differentiation moat this page underwrites may also be a price ceiling. **(2) Capex is stepping up ~80% to the high-₩40tn range and is being pulled in, with no buyback despite large net cash** (Culpium; Jefferies) — landing in the same week Jefferies research published **"HAS MEMORY PEAKED?": price hikes tracking 15-20% vs 25-30% expected, memory prices peaking ~1H27, and 2027 consensus requiring ~US$2tn of US AI capex to be achievable**, with **HBM4 costs jumping to US$31-32/GB**. That is the bear case in one line — peak-margin cash committed to capacity into a forecast price peak. It is also consistent with management's own long-standing argument that ~70% OP margins are "not high enough" to fund the greenfield/hybrid-bonding step-up. **Tape context (not a company event, but it dominated the reaction):** on 2026-07-27 the **KOSPI fell 10.7% with a sell sidecar and the year's eighth circuit breaker, Samsung -12%, SK Hynix -13%**, driven by **CXMT's IPO debut at +466% — China's second-largest IPO ever — "making the memory threat feel suddenly very real"** plus a live-Fed-hike scare (~40% odds priced). On the print the stock opened **-6% pre-market and recovered to -2.3%**, while **[[MU]] fell a further ~4% after hours on top of -8.9% intraday**. Also logged: an unverified @zerohedge claim that SK Hynix slumped below a US IPO price — **flagged, not adopted**, as it is uncorroborated elsewhere in this ingest; and a Korean-press exclusive (@jukan05) that **LPDDR6 mass production starts in 2H with Xiaomi as first customer**, which sits in tension with the same-day comment that PC/mobile volumes are being rationed. Cross-filed [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md), [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).

| 07-27 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "Global Memory, NVIDIA & Broadcom: Quick thoughts on strategic partnerships" (PRIMARY PDF, 16pp; ingested 2026-08-11)** | supply | **bull** | 🔴 **THE PRIMARY OF THE DESK RELAY IMMEDIATELY BELOW — ingested two weeks late (the file was locked on P: for three consecutive runs). Everything the relay carried was verified and is NOT repeated here; what follows is what the relay left out.** **① THE RATING MARK, absent from the relay: Outperform, PT KRW 3,300,000** (px KRW1,781,000 at the 24-Jul close, +542% relative performance), on **6.2x forward 5Q-8Q EPS of KRW536,158** — the same 6.2x multiple Bernstein applies to [[SAMSUNG]], so the relative call between the two Korean names is pure EPS, not multiple. **EPS: KRW60,341 (2025A) → 395,677 (2026E) → 568,862 (2027E)**, i.e. 29.5x / 4.5x / 3.1x reported P/E — a 3.1x 2027 P/E is the sharpest statement of the "market does not believe these earnings" problem this page tracks. **② THE DEAL DETAIL THE RELAY COMPRESSED TO "$500B+": the LOIs cover memory AND "a 2GW VERA RUBIN DSX AI FACTORY PLANNED TO BE ONLINE FOR SK TELECOM IN 2027."** ⚠️ **That is a dated, sized, named-offtaker datapoint and it was missing from this page — a 2GW facility for the affiliate telco, live in 2027, is both an [[NVDA]] revenue line and a captive demand sink for SK hynix's own HBM. The memory leg is described as "long-term technical developments & stable supply of next-gen AI memory (HBM4 & later?)" — the question mark is Bernstein's — "to enable SK hynix to 'expand the foundation for growth.'"** 🔴 **③ NET-NEW AND NOT ON THIS PAGE IN ANY FORM: "SK Group reportedly will ADDITIONALLY collaborate with other global tech companies for memory supply worth US$250B over the next 5 years."** ⚠️ **Sourcing is "reportedly" with two press links, not disclosure — carry it as a press report. But if it stands, the group's contracted memory book is $500B+ (NVDA) plus $250B (others) across five years, and the second leg has no named counterparties at all. That is the single largest unattributed demand commitment on this page.** **④ The rating logic across the complex, which is where Bernstein differs from the Street: Outperform on Samsung, SK hynix AND [[MU]], Underperform on [[KIOXIA]] — and the discriminator is explicitly the China threat by product. "We however worry about the long-term threat from China in NAND and thus rate KIOXIA Underperform. The threat is much lower in DRAM as CHINA EVENTUALLY WILL FIND IT DIFFICULT TO COMPETE IN DRAM MARKET WITHOUT EUV."** ➜ **That is a litho-access argument, not a capacity argument, and it is the cleanest available test of the CXMT debate on this page: it says CXMT's wafer ramp is not the binding question — its node ceiling is. Hold it against the CXMT capacity marks already here (UBS 240K→466K wpm; JPM 16% capacity / 11% bit share by 28E off a reported 600K wfpm ambition), which measure the thing Bernstein says does not decide the outcome.** **⑤ Industry frame: consensus memory revenue ~US$0.9T (CY2026) → US$1.3T (CY2027) → US$1.3T (CY2028) — the relay carried the $1.3T; the CY26 base and the FLATNESS from '27 to '28 are new, and the flatness is the more interesting half. Exhibit 3 splits it across Samsung Memory / SK hynix / Micron / SanDisk / KIOXIA / CXMT / YMTC, with CXMT and YMTC on Bernstein's own estimates.** **⑥ Risks as stated: "an earlier end of favorable pricing environment, which can be a result of weaker demand or higher supply," investor sentiment/valuation, and China's memory progress "especially in NAND."** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Stacy Rasgon / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai / Alrick Shaw / Arpad von Nemes / Eva Zhang, 2026-07-27; first published 26 Jul 2026 21:30 UTC) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260726_Bernstein_NVDA_Global_Memory-_NVIDIA_-_Broadcom-_Quick_thoughts_on_stra.html) |

| 07-24 | SemiAnalysis "Can AMD break the CUDA Moat? AMD Advancing AI 2026" | produto | **mixed** | **The HBM4 pin-speed bake-off, the 2027 HBM-price call, and the de-spec that offsets it — all read off the buyer's BOM.** **(1) Pin speed / binning:** AMD's MI455X carries **12 HBM4 stacks / 432GB / 23.3 TB/s** vs Nvidia Rubin and Google at **8 stacks / 288GB**, Rubin **22 TB/s** — a near-tie despite AMD's **50% wider bus**, because AMD runs **7.6 Gbps** pin speed vs Nvidia's **10.7 Gbps (40% faster)**. "Nvidia aggressively raised its targeted HBM4 pin speeds last year, well above the original JEDEC HBM4 specification… precisely driven by the need to make up for the memory bandwidth deficit to AMD MI455X." That "was challenging for memory suppliers to keep up with. Memory suppliers had to rework their HBM4 to deliver the spec Nvidia was targeting. This also pushed out upstream output for Rubin **but as of today these issues have been finally resolved**" — and **Nvidia must use a much higher-quality bin than AMD**. AMD + Nvidia are the only two shipping HBM4 this year. Net for the #1 HBM supplier: HBM4 is **two markets** (easy 7.6 Gbps AMD bin vs premium 10.7 Gbps Nvidia bin), so the differentiator shifts toward **yield on the top bin** and the quality/price spread between suppliers — a share/mix question, not a volume one. Cross-check, held carefully: this page's own SemiAnalysis datapoint has SK hynix's **HBM4 engineering samples at ~10 Gbps with lower jitter / stronger package execution than Samsung's** ("Memory Mania," 02-06), just under the 10.7 Gbps Rubin target — **SemiAnalysis names no bin winner and neither do we.** Also the first explicit statement on the page that the Rubin HBM4 upstream-output delay is *behind* the chain. **(2) Bull — 2027 pricing:** expects "a significant increase in HBM pricing in 2027 as memory suppliers move to match the margins they now earn on commodity DRAM," strong enough that AMD "can't pass the full memory increase on to its customers" → **gross-margin drag on the MI455X package**; "HBM, DRAM, and NAND prices alike have increased dramatically since our baseline in mid-2025." Direct external support for management's refusal to commit to HBM-DDR margin parity (roadshow 07-06). **(3) Bear — de-spec:** original 2025 MI455X BOM carried **138TB of DRAM (LPDDR5X + DDR5 MRDIMM)**; current estimate has **LPDDR5X removed entirely** (the up-to-**1TB** per-accelerator direct-attached LPDDR tier is gone on tight supply) plus a significant DDR5 RDIMM de-spec — "**much of the DRAM price increases are offset by aggressive de-specs.**" [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_ENC_Can_AMD_break_the_CUDA_Moat_AMD_Advancing_AI_2026_email.html) |
| 07-21 | SemiAnalysis "Meta's Infrastructure Team Needs A Culture Reset" | demanda | **bear** | **A quantified HBM-content cut at one of AMD's two largest GPU customers — the sharpest instance of the de-spec risk.** Meta's custom MI450/MI455 variant **halves the compute silicon (8 dies → 4) and cuts HBM from 12 stacks to 6 per package**, and **downgrades the HBM4 itself from 12-Hi to 8-Hi**. SemiAnalysis argues the design "is going to nuke AMD's volume at Meta because TBD will vastly prefer Rubin if the half MI455 design is chosen," so the memory read is **doubly negative: fewer HBM stacks per package on a program that may itself shrink**. Logged for this page because the swing factor for 2027 HBM revenue is **bits per accelerator**, which most models hold flat — and because it is the uncovered (non-LTA) portion of the book where content cuts bite. (Cross-filed on [themes/hbm-memory.md](themes/hbm-memory.md).) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_21_ENC_Metas_Infrastructure_Team_Needs_A_Culture_Reset_email.html) |

| 07-25 | Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini (Technology Hardware weekly, Jul 19-25) | supply | mixed | **"US Negotiating Leverage Bears Fruit" — the sharpest articulation yet of the political mechanism behind Korean memory supply, and it has a bearish tail.** SIG (Neutral, ₩1,781,000) argues the US administration's negotiating strategy is working: **Korean memory suppliers are now expected to unveil major US memory-fab investment plans** (Korean president in Silicon Valley for meetings and an AI summit; Tom's Hardware reporting Samsung and SK Hynix set to announce large deals with leading US tech firms). Hosseini says the leverage rests on two points: **(1) the majority of Korean suppliers' memory demand comes from US-based customers, >50% of revenue mix**; and **(2) the rise of CXMT and YMTC as a viable supply alternative**. Both Chinese makers are **currently barred from supplying US-based OEMs** and both sit on the **Pentagon's Section 1260H list**, with **YMTC also on the Commerce Entity List** — so the implicit threat is that Washington can "unlock" that supply. **SIG's sizing, net-new: CXMT and YMTC reach 17% and 25% of DRAM and NAND supply respectively by YE28.** The consequence he draws is the part that matters for the cycle debate on this page: **new US greenfield memory fabs cannot come online before 2029** given construction and tooling lead times, so **in the interim CXMT/YMTC "could still offer a valuable short-term remedy to a tight memory supply-demand balance"** — the US "essentially killing two birds with one stone" by easing the imbalance and reducing memory-price inflation. Read as a **policy-driven downside scenario to the pricing curve** that is independent of demand: the tightness that underwrites current DRAM/NAND ASPs is partly a function of an export-control regime that the same administration has an incentive to relax once the fab commitments are banked. Cross-ref [SAMSUNG.md](SAMSUNG.md), [MU.md](MU.md), [themes](themes/). (Susquehanna · Mehdi Hosseini / Bastien Faucon-Morin, 2026-07-25) |
